# 501: Chapter 502: I’m the strongest of Kanto’s Elite Four!

"The first one is Blue, huh? This guy isn't an easy opponent at all."

Archer stared at the first portrait to stop—Kanto's Elite Four member, Blue.

No doubt about it—this match was going to be high-level.

Then again…

There wasn't any match in the top sixteen that wasn't high-level.

"Yeah, that guy's a handful."

Leaf toyed with one of her braided pigtails, glancing at the portrait on the big screen.

Blue—his name even sounded a bit like hers. But Leaf didn't have much affection for him.

Over the past six months, she'd fought him several times. He challenged her and knocked her out of the champion tier; then she challenged him, knocked him down, and then You and a few others joined in, causing the seventh and eighth spots to flip constantly.

"No idea who he'll be matched with."

Leaf watched the spinning portraits, which were gradually slowing down.

Soon, a baseball cap appeared.

The arena erupted at once, and Blue lifted a brow—because the person who appeared was Red!

Blue vs. Red!

Blue instinctively looked in Red's direction—just in time to see Red lift his head, crimson eyes burning beneath the brim of his hat.

'We finally meet on the battlefield!'

Blue understood that gaze instantly. A strange thrill surged through him, curling his lips into a fiery, arrogant grin at his childhood friend.

"Oh-ho~ this'll be fun to watch."

Nemona grinned mischievously. Blue and Red—Kanto's two "monsters," and fellow members of the Kanto Elite Four.

This was a Kanto civil war!

"Eh?! Why is it Red vs. Blue!?"

Ash—fresh off his Silver Conference run—gaped at the board.

"It's not that weird. It's already the top sixteen. But Blue's totally gonna win," Gary shrugged.

"What are you talking about, jerk?! There's no way Red loses!" Ash snapped back, firmly on his brother's side.

"Blue's gonna win!"

"No, Red's gonna win!"

"BLUE!!"

"RED!!"

The two bickered like angry roosters.

"…Unbelievable."

Misty stared at them, exasperated. Their bickering was childish—but she kind of understood. Those two childhood friends had always been like this.

"Time really flies… Red's already made it this far."

Misty looked at the two portraits, her voice soft with emotion.

Last year, they were watching Dylan and Red—both still "raw"—battle each other. Now in the blink of an eye, Dylan was the world's strongest trainer…

And Red was standing here in the top sixteen.

It felt surreal.

Thanks to Ash, Red also counted as their friend. Sort of.

"Yeah, but it's not surprising." Brock nodded, unsurprised.

He'd always known Red was destined for the top.

"Now, let's welcome our competitors to the stage!"

The host received the signal and shouted passionately.

"Blue!"

"Red!"

From both ends of the stage tunnel, two silhouettes stepped out.

The crowd erupted into deafening cheers.

Red, as always, wore his cap, blue jeans, and a red-and-white jacket—simple.

Across from him, Blue walked out with one hand in his pocket—black fitted turtleneck, purple pants—taking his place with cool confidence.

Their gazes locked the moment Blue looked up.

"Blue and Red, huh…"

Dylan chuckled to himself.

Fate. These two were destined to clash eventually.

The host flipped through his cue cards and smiled knowingly.

"I'm sure everyone here knows these two. Both Blue and Red are members of the Kanto Elite Four."

"Both are incredibly skilled trainers, but many might not know this—they left Pallet Town on the same day last year."

"And more importantly—they're childhood friends!"

The crowd gasped.

"Rumor has it they've been rivals since childhood, and that didn't change even after both received their starters. They battled throughout their journey, trading wins, growing stronger, and eventually both becoming Elite Four members."

The host grinned.

"But today, they may finally settle things—here on the world championship stage. Let's see who will enter the top eight!"

He finished dramatically. Though honestly, he suspected Red had the higher chance.

Both the Elite Four ranking and the championship seeding placed Red above Blue.

"Blue's no match for Red, right?"

Leaf asked Dylan.

"Red is stronger," Dylan replied simply.

"But Blue isn't without a chance. Their gap isn't as wide as people think."

He pulled out snacks, ready to enjoy this classic Red vs. Blue showdown.

Realistically, Dylan knew Red had the advantage—better raw strength, perfect focus, calm execution. In evenly matched battles, mistakes decided the outcome.

Red barely made mistakes.

Blue's chances were… not great.

But Blue himself clearly didn't agree.

The referee announced the rules and stepped back. The pointer on the display stopped on Blue—he would send his Pokémon out first.

Blue flicked his Poké Ball lightly, not throwing it yet. Instead, he looked at Red.

"So the day's finally here, huh, Red."

With a cocky grin, he addressed his childhood rival.

Red silently watched him.

Yep—Blue's doing the thing again.

…He was starting to show off.

"Look at this, Red. We're standing in a stadium filled with roaring crowds. And now I'm gonna beat you with my own two hands! I planned this whole scene out last year!"

Blue swept his gaze across the Indigo Arena, then smirked sideways at Red.

Red: "…"

"But we both became Elite Four members, so that dream couldn't come true. This world championship stage, though? It's even better than the Indigo League."

"Beating you here will feel just as great!"

His grin widened—arrogant and blazing.

"When I saw I'd been matched with you, do you know how happy I was?"

"Red, you're my rival! As a rival, I suppose I can barely acknowledge your strength. Which means only I get to defeat you!"

Red's eyes flickered—he didn't expect Blue of all people to say "acknowledge your strength."

Blue never admitted anything like that.

Ever.

"I won't lose to you."

Red lowered his brim, speaking calmly.

"Tch~ Calm as always. Makes it more fun."

Blue tilted his head, raised his Poké Ball—

"Red, today I'll show you the gap between us! I, Blue Oak—the genius Pokémon Trainer!"

"I've found the perfect Pokémon! With them, I'll reach the peak!"

Head held high, he declared with breathtaking arrogance.

Red: "…"

"Alright, Red—time to show you who'll be the world's strongest! Stronger than anyone else!"

"Beat you! Beat Selene! Beat Giovanni! I am the strongest of Kanto's Elite Four!"

Yes—Blue fully believed it.

Dylan? He didn't count as human—he was heavenly, not earthly.

This world championship was the perfect stage.

Red: "…"

Finally done.

Red felt exhausted already. Before every battle, Blue always talked too much.

Especially considering Blue's win rate against him had already dropped to around 30%.

Where did he get the confidence to brag like this?

I'm Champion-tier No. 2. What rank are you again?

But Red didn't bother talking.

His specialty was beating up people who bragged in front of him.

Especially if that person was Blue.

And especially after Blue had just done a huge bragging performance.

Blue opened with Exeggutor. He liked using it against Red's usual Blastoise lead—its Psychic typing was versatile.

Red calmly sent out his ace—Roaring Moon.

The ancient paradox dragon spread its wings with a thunderous roar.

Blue immediately switched out, sending in Tyranitar.

"Come, Red! Today I'll show you my full power!"

Blue raised his head proudly.

"Not only you, but Selene and Giovanni too—I'm the strongest of Kanto's Elite Four!"

(End of chapter)

---

---

---

# 502: Chapter 503: Damn It!

"Blue really is… heh."

Giovanni couldn't help but chuckle twice and shake his head as he heard Blue's bold declaration from the stands.

Young people were always brash—he understood.

And someone as proud and fiery as Blue was practically an endangered species.

"Still, I wonder if he can actually pull it off. Red is really strong."

Selene giggled. She didn't think Blue could beat Red. Rankings weren't everything, but they did mean something.

And Blue's ranking wasn't as high as Red's.

The battle began, and of course, Blue and Red's fight instantly became a fierce, deadlocked clash.

Tyranitar and Roaring Moon were both extremely powerful Pokémon—and even more so after being raised by these two.

They knew each other too well, so both Red and Blue were fighting at full focus.

But in the end, Red seized the opportunity and used Roaring Moon to defeat Tyranitar.

Blue's expression darkened, but he recovered quickly and sent out Arcanine, successfully finishing off the weakened Roaring Moon.

But Arcanine took heavy damage in return, and the momentum began to shift.

Red sent out Snorlax as his second Pokémon. Blue kept Arcanine in, trying to pressure with Extreme Speed.

Red played steadily. Eventually, Snorlax caught Arcanine during an opening, landed a crushing hit, then followed up with Earthquake to knock it out.

Blue switched to Exeggutor. Red kept Snorlax in. Snorlax went down, but Exeggutor was heavily injured.

Red's third Pokémon was Tornadus—one of Unova's legendary Forces of Nature, captured back when Red and his three teammates traveled there.

Beep—Tornadus (Incarnate Forme), Flying-type, Cyclone Pokémon

Ability: Prankster

Moves: Leaf Tornado, Hurricane, Hammer Arm, Extrasensory, Swagger…

Info: Said to cause storms by whipping the atmosphere. Its humanoid appearance is just a disguise, not its true body (Incarnate Forme).

Red used the Incarnate form instead of the Therian form. Giovanni found the Reveal Glass, which let the Forms of Nature change to their true Therian forms.

But Red preferred the Incarnate form because of its Prankster ability—it was stronger in his eyes.

"Tornadus, huh? Then… Thundurus!"

Blue withdrew Exeggutor and threw out his fourth Pokémon.

It looked somewhat dragon-like—blue upper body with purple spots, purple lower body, and a tail lined with black iron-ball-like beads.

Therian Thundurus!

Beep—Thundurus (Therian Forme), Electric/Flying-type, Bolt Strike Pokémon

Ability: Volt Absorb

Moves: Wildbolt Storm, Thunder, Discharge, Volt Switch, Rain Dance…

Info: Appears amid thunder. It rains down lightning from the beads on its tail, wiping out foes with overwhelming power (Therian Forme).

The appearance of two legendary Pokémon sent the stadium into a frenzy.

"Tornadus vs. Thundurus?! This is the top sixteen!?"

"Worth every coin! Even if I die here, seeing this live was worth the ticket!"

"Why must there be a winner?! Kanto fans just want all four Elite Four members in the top eight!"

"I don't even know who to cheer for!"

"Just cheer! LET'S GOOO!!"

The arena was boiling with excitement.

The fight was already intense—but with the incarnate and therian legends on the field, it peaked.

"Damn it!! Those are our legends!"

Iris nearly cried. Those were Unova's own legends!

"Dylan catching Reshiram and Kyurem was one thing, but these Elite Four are actual bandits—they took the entire quartet of Forces of Nature!"

Her heart hurt. Kanto trainers were bandits!

Why did they all come to rip off Unova!? What did Unova even have left?

Only the Swords of Justice—and if someone from another region caught them too, it'd be absurd.

Their legendary heritage was being used against them.

"Legendary Pokémon come down to fate. Nothing to be done," Alder said calmly. Meeting legends and catching them was individual destiny—unforced and unpredictable.

He was more curious which was stronger—Tornadus or Thundurus.

The battle resumed. Both Red and Blue Terastallized.

Tornadus went Flying Tera, Thundurus went Electric Tera.

No doubt Tornadus had the type disadvantage. But Red didn't switch.

Thundurus clearly had more firepower—its special attack was monstrous, and Blue had raised it well.

But battles weren't pure stat contests. Red used Tornadus expertly.

With Prankster, Tornadus successfully used Swagger to confuse Thundurus—and used that opening to take it down.

Blue's face went green.

But Tornadus was also in bad shape. Blue's fifth Pokémon, Rhyperior, defeated it at some cost.

Red sent out Lapras. Blue swapped Rhyperior out and sent Exeggutor back in.

But Exeggutor still fell to Lapras. Blue was pushed to his limits.

Letting Rhyperior fight Lapras would be suicide—four-time weakness to Water.

Blue had only his ace left—Blastoise. He Mega Evolved it.

Red instantly countered with his own ace—Charizard—Mega evolving into Charizard X.

Charizard X was Fire/Dragon, not weak to Water. Neither side had a direct advantage, though Water still resisted Fire.

If Mega Blastoise couldn't beat Charizard X, Blue was finished.

Rhyperior would never get past Lapras.

"I won't lose! There's no way I lose here!!"

Blue gritted his teeth. He couldn't accept it.

The situation was bad, but he believed he had a chance. He was Blue Oak!

How could he fall here? This was just the top sixteen—and his opponent was Red.

"Blue, you don't stand a chance against me."

Red's crimson eyes glowed beneath his brim.

"What crap are you spouting?! Red, I haven't lost yet!"

Blue glared fiercely. His pride refused to accept such "nonsense."

"Watch closely, Red! Watch how hard I struggle to keep winning!"

I will win!

My desire for victory will carry me forward!

And then—

"…Impossible!!"

Seeing Rhyperior collapse, Blue's face turned pale.

Rhyperior fell—and Mega Blastoise had lost to Charizard X.

He lost. Completely. Despite struggling with everything he had.

"Damn it! It ends just like this?! Losing in a full-power battle?! I finally made it this far…"

Blue staggered back, staring at his trembling hand in despair.

He lost. To Red.

This wasn't how it was supposed to be—his era wasn't even supposed to begin yet.

"Rhyperior is unable to battle. Lapras wins."

The referee announced.

"Blue has lost all six Pokémon. Red wins! Red advances!"

The stadium exploded into applause and cheers.

"We have a victor! After an intense clash, Red defeats his powerful rival Blue and becomes the first trainer to enter the top eight!"

The host boomed.

"As expected, Blue wasn't Red's match." Giovanni clapped lightly, unsurprised.

"What a battle… He's grown even stronger. A terrifying rival," Leon murmured, watching the red-eyed boy.

He'd fought Red before—and lost by a hair, his ranking taken.

But Leon believed he'd reclaim his spot someday.

"With one Kanto powerhouse down, hopefully they keep fighting each other," Hop laughed energetically.

Kanto was too strong—too many monsters. The pressure on other regions was insane.

But the matchup algorithm was doing wonders—last round Giovanni fought his own son, and now Red and Blue fought like brothers.

The matching system really knew what it was doing.

Who knew what surprises came next?

Leon smiled—better to prepare fully rather than rely on luck.

On the field—

Blue was still dazed, unable to recover.

Of course—someone as proud as Blue being eliminated before top eight, and by his childhood rival, no less… it was a devastating blow.

It hit him as hard as the time he became Champion and immediately got dethroned by Red before he'd even warmed the seat.

Blue was spiraling, even wondering if his training methods were wrong. Why couldn't he win?

Footsteps approached.

Blue looked up—and saw Red.

"Tch—!"

Blue quickly straightened up and looked away.

"I don't wanna admit it, but you did get lucky and beat me this time."

Blue muttered irritably. Losing hurt badly, but he recovered quickly.

And unlike the game version, this Blue had decent mental strength—thanks to a certain someone's "training," and the many powerful people surrounding him.

Red: "…"

"But just you wait! Next time! Next time I won't slip up—I'm not losing to you again!"

(End of chapter)

---

---

# 503: Chapter 504: Before the Power of Dragons

The first trainer to enter the top eight was decided—Kanto's Elite Four member, Red.

This result wasn't surprising. Red's strength had already been made clear during the ranking phase.

And for many spectators, this outcome was actually preferable.

Because Red eliminated Blue. Blue was also an extremely tough opponent. Having one of them knocked out through an internal matchup was the best possible scenario.

Of course, that was only how the other regions felt. Fans from Kanto's region certainly did not think so.

All they felt was pain. In their eyes, the ideal outcome was for all six Kanto-region trainers to make it into the top eight.

Even if that idea wasn't realistic—six people from the same region avoiding each other entirely was way too hard. Internal matches were inevitable.

After the first match finished, a thirty-minute intermission passed, and the second round of matchup selections began.

"Please! Please don't let it be another internal match!"

"Yeah! We already had one last round. The system can't keep targeting Kanto-region like this, right? If it happens again, there's definitely a conspiracy!"

"Lmao, this is your home turf. This is the Indigo Arena, remember? What conspiracy?"

"Exactly! Haha—who told your region to send so many monsters?"

"I strongly support more Kanto-region internal matches! Free up some slots for the rest of us! There aren't enough to go around!"

The audience whispered among themselves, and the livestream chat was the same.

Kanto's overwhelming strength naturally became the butt of jokes from every other region.

After all, out of only sixteen spots, Kanto-region claimed six. Ridiculous.

But that was normal. Kanto was where the Pokémon League HQ was located—of course they'd be strong.

On the big screen, the trainer portraits spun rapidly, drawing everyone's eyes.

"I wonder if it will be my brother this time…"

Lillie looked a little excited. She loved watching her brother battle—his calm, confident presence on the field captivated her.

And standing on that stage, her brother was the focus of the entire world, wasn't he?

Someone that strong deserved to stun everyone.

"It doesn't matter. Early or late, my turn will come." Dylan chuckled softly. He truly didn't care.

There were eight matches in total—well, now seven left. He would face his battle sooner or later.

Before long, the first portrait slowly settled.

A sharp crew cut, a lean face, thin lips lifting into a composed, mature smile.

"Huh? It's Uncle Giovanni." Selene blinked.

"My turn?" Giovanni's interest sparked as he looked toward the other portrait. Who would be his opponent?

He glanced briefly at Dylan—so long as it wasn't Dylan, Giovanni was confident he could defeat anyone.

"Giovanni… that's not an easy opponent." Cynthia's golden eyes grew serious.

"Yes. Giovanni is one of the most dangerous people among the remaining competitors. And from what I've heard… he spent nearly half a year in seclusion training. He should be even stronger now."

Beside her, Caitlin spoke in calm, measured tones.

Ever since Giovanni failed in his ranking challenge against Lance half a year ago, he had been training nonstop.

"And although there's no formal proof, Giovanni definitely captured one of the Forces of Nature."

Caitlin continued. As Unova's Elite Four, she naturally had access to information.

Cynthia and Dawn both frowned slightly.

"Red has Tornadus. Blue has Thundurus. Selene has Enamorus. That leaves the last Force of Nature—Landorus. It must be with Giovanni."

Caitlin's gaze flickered as she stared at Giovanni's portrait.

Half a year ago, right after his loss to Lance, Giovanni left with Red and the others for Unova. They stayed several days.

Unova officials were confused at first—why were these four suddenly visiting?

Then, in the ranking phase, the three boys each revealed a Force of Nature.

And Unova realized the truth.

These four had come specifically to capture their region's legendary gods!

Damn it. Those Pokémon were sacred to Unova's culture for generations.

And since Red, Blue, and Selene had already shown theirs, there was no reason to believe Giovanni—leader of the four—didn't have one.

"Yes… Landorus is a Ground-type deity. Perfectly aligned with Giovanni's specialty." Dawn said, voice tightening.

"He'll be incredibly difficult to defeat. If he gets matched with Champion Dylan, that'd be ideal."

Caitlin tilted her head, smiling lightly.

She didn't qualify for the knockout bracket—but Cynthia did. And a Giovanni vs. Dylan matchup would benefit both Sinnoh and her workplace, Unova.

Cynthia and Dawn responded with helpless smiles.

At that moment, the second portrait settled.

Everyone froze.

A head of wild, blood-red hair. A sharp, cold face.

Johto's "Dragon Master"—Champion Lance.

"Oh my… Lance?"

Selene blinked, startled, then shot Giovanni a playful look.

"Uncle Giovanni, feeling confident? You didn't beat Champion Lance last time."

"…This is…"

Giovanni stared at Lance's portrait for several seconds.

Then his lips slowly curled upward.

"…the perfect matchup."

Something fierce burned in Giovanni's eyes.

Lance.

The one who subdued him on Suicune Island.

The one he failed to surpass during the ranking challenge.

Why did he challenge Lance immediately after locating the Reveal Glass?

Because he wanted to know—at that moment, where he stood compared to Lance.

The result needed no explanation. Without his Mega Beedrill, he fell short.

But after half a year of training, he was no longer the same.

"Tsk. Rare to see you fired up, old man."

Even Blue paused his brooding to glance at Giovanni.

The intensity of Giovanni's aura was impossible to ignore.

To Blue, Giovanni was the model senior—calm, rational, steady, immensely powerful.

He shared his ground-type knowledge generously.

But honestly… Blue always felt Giovanni was too calm. No passion.

But now? Giovanni radiated pure battle-lust.

"Because it's Lance." Giovanni replied coolly, expression returning to composed normalcy.

Across the arena—

"So it's Giovanni. Interesting."

Karen crossed her legs, examining her newly done nails as she glanced at Lance.

"Champion Lance, any thoughts?" she asked with a bright smile.

"A defeated challenger." Lance replied blandly.

"But he didn't lose easily. And he should be stronger now."

After losing to Lance, Giovanni essentially locked himself away for training. Everyone knew that.

"Maybe he's been waiting all this time for a rematch—to wash away that loss."

"It doesn't matter."

Lance's voice was cold. Arms crossed, posture straight, the sharpness of a drawn blade radiating off him.

"Before the absolute power of dragons, he has no chance."

His tone was steady, as if stating an undeniable truth.

"Ahh, that confidence~ That's the Lance I know." Karen laughed. She wasn't surprised at all.

After working with Lance for a year, she understood one thing—this man was "edgy arrogance incarnate."

Three sentences never left dragons. Five sentences never left his "dragon legion."

People said Blue was a cocky loudmouth—but Karen felt he was still a tier below Lance.

If these two ever met and talked, that would be fun to watch.

Karen smiled happily and leaned back, popping a cherry into her mouth.

Being knocked out in the round of thirty-two didn't bother her—she was just a Johto Elite Four member. Why stress?

A cushy job, a great life. All thanks to Champion Dylan. Maybe she should send him something spicy tonight?

Last time she sent him a picture of herself in lace lingerie, he responded with a thumbs-up emoji.

Yes. Even Dylan liked beautiful women.

"Let's give a warm welcome to the trainers of this match!"

The host's booming voice echoed across the stadium.

"Giovanni!!"

"Champion Lance!!"

As cheers erupted, two figures emerged from the tunnels—Giovanni in his black suit, Lance in his fitted outfit and sweeping cape.

"Ugh—why another internal match?!"

"Not really internal—Lance is Johto's champion now. But still feels weird seeing them fight."

"Champion Lance will win! The dragon legion is unbeatable!"

"Unbeatable? Selene already beat him once. Giovanni might win too."

"Bruh, Selene is Selene and Giovanni is Giovanni. Half a year ago he lost—did you forget?"

"That was half a year ago. Giovanni's been grinding nonstop. Not beating Lance then doesn't mean he can't now."

The livestream chat erupted in debate.

On the field, the two remained unmoved.

"Heh… should I thank the system for delivering you to me, Champion Lance?"

Giovanni loosened his neck slightly, a faint smile on his lips as he stared at Lance.

"You should feel regret. This is where your journey ends."

Lance responded coolly.

Giovanni annoyed him—not personally, but because of that one line he would never forget.

"Lance, born evil."

Even if their positions had been opposed during the Suicune Island incident, Giovanni's words had left a mark.

And Lance had never shaken the discomfort he felt around him.

"Still as arrogant as ever. But have you considered… maybe your journey ends here today?"

Giovanni lowered his eyes and chuckled.

"You? Stop me?"

Lance responded flatly.

"You've already lost to my dragons once."

His tone didn't shift. He spoke as though declaring the obvious.

"That was then. You're too conceited, Lance."

Giovanni's eyes narrowed.

Today—he would defeat Lance.

The referee between them was sweating through his uniform, unsure whether he should speak.

The air felt like it might explode.

"Conceited? Ridiculous."

Lance gave a cold laugh, raising his chin slightly. Under the curtain of blood-red hair, his eyes glinted with regal disdain.

"I've told you before, Giovanni."

"Ants on the ground will never threaten dragons soaring through the sky."

"No matter how many times you come, the outcome is the same. My dragon legion cannot lose to your ground types."

Giovanni chuckled softly.

Lance was the same as ever. Defeating him would be satisfying.

"Let's see if you can still say that afterward."

Giovanni pulled out a Poké Ball.

"Hmph."

Lance's lips curled into a feral grin.

"How amusing. You're that confident? Then allow me to break you completely."

"Witness the reborn dragon legion!"

The referee nearly cried.

He felt completely unnecessary.

(End of chapter)

---

---

# 504: Chapter 506: You Are the Challenger

The battle between Mega Kangaskhan and Mega Gyarados was extremely intense. After all, both Mega Kangaskhan and Mega Gyarados were experts in close combat.

But as the battle went on, Mega Gyarados gradually fell into disadvantage.

When Mega Gyarados once again used Aqua Tail to block Mega Kangaskhan's Drain Punch and braced itself with full force, the baby Kangaskhan suddenly rushed in from the side, leaping high. With its fist glowing white, it smashed it hard onto Mega Gyarados's head.

Fighting-type Drain Punch was super effective against Mega Gyarados, who gained a Dark typing after Mega Evolution.

Though the baby Kangaskhan wasn't as strong as its mother, its strike still hurt Mega Gyarados.

Mega Gyarados groaned in pain, forcing power outward to knock Mega Kangaskhan back, then turned toward the baby Kangaskhan and roared in fury.

But the baby Kangaskhan didn't linger—it quickly retreated to its mother's side, baring its teeth at Mega Gyarados.

Mega Gyarados panted heavily, slowly rising into the air, its fierce eyes locked on the two Kangaskhan before it.

The two sides faced off once more.

"Feels like Mega Gyarados is really falling behind."

"It's obvious. Mega Kangaskhan used to be Giovanni's ace. Besides…"

"Two versus one is so shameless! This is the World Championships—a fair competition! How can a Pokémon like that be allowed?!"

"What shameless? Mega Evolution is completely legal and fair. What now, you can't beat it so you want Mega Kangaskhan banned?"

"The ideal plan is to let Mega Gyarados wear down Mega Kangaskhan, then Dragonite comes in to finish it."

Spectators everywhere discussed excitedly—that's the joy of competitive battles.

"Mega Gyarados isn't Mega Kangaskhan's match. Lance isn't switching it out yet?" Wallace looked puzzled.

The match had been going on for a while. Despite having aerial advantage, Mega Gyarados clearly couldn't overcome Mega Kangaskhan.

Mega Kangaskhan's raw power was already no weaker than Mega Gyarados, not to mention it had the baby assisting beside it.

Though the baby could only mimic Mega Kangaskhan's moves due to the Mega energy that "accelerated its growth," the 2-on-1 advantage was still overwhelming.

"Although tough, there are still openings—and Mega Gyarados can be used to exhaust Mega Kangaskhan." Steven speculated.

Mega Kangaskhan was extremely strong. If Lance didn't want to use his ace Dragonite too early, he had to sacrifice Mega Gyarados.

Now that Giovanni had a new ace, preserving the main Dragonite's condition became necessary.

The battle continued. After revealing some move information, Mega Kangaskhan managed to defeat Mega Gyarados.

Lance lost the first point.

Giovanni withdrew Mega Kangaskhan instead of letting it continue fighting.

Both sent out their third Pokémon.

Lance sent out Salamence, Giovanni sent out Rhyperior.

This time, Lance altered his strategy—favoring ranged special attacks instead of close-combat pressure.

Rhyperior was quadruple-weak to Water—even with Solid Rock, Hydro Pump from Salamence hit brutally hard.

Rhyperior fell, though Salamence suffered heavy damage as well.

Giovanni then sent out Great Tusk as his fourth Pokémon, taking advantage of the opening to defeat Salamence.

Lance responded with Naganadel—the Ultra Beast he had once obtained from Gus.

Beep—Naganadel. Dragon + Poison-type. Poison Needle Pokémon.

Ability: Beast Boost

Moves: Dragon Pulse, Toxic, Nasty Plot, Sludge Wave, Air Cutter…

Info: An Ultra Beast that fires sticky, luminous poison from its stinger.

"This one is going to be troublesome for Giovanni."

Watching Naganadel, Mizuki raised a brow.

A Poison + Dragon typing gave Naganadel great resistances. Though weak to Ground, Naganadel could fly.

Fast and powerful, it was the definition of a high-speed cannon, with no need to fight Great Tusk up close.

Unlike Salamence, which, being a mixed attacker with higher physical attack, inevitably got baited into close combat by Lance.

"Yeah… hard to deal with."

Blue nodded. Giovanni specialized in Ground-types—very few on his roster could fly.

Giovanni let Great Tusk fight for a bit, but it was hopeless. It even ate a powerful Dragon Pulse.

Giovanni promptly withdrew it and sent out his fifth Pokémon—the flying-capable Gliscor.

Another fierce battle followed, but Gliscor still fell to Naganadel. The Ultra Beast's strength was exceptional.

However, Gliscor did its job, leaving Naganadel heavily injured.

Naganadel's bulk was terrible. Giovanni then sent out Nidoking and traded blows, forcing a mutual KO.

At this point, Giovanni had lost Nidoking, Rhyperior, and Gliscor. Lance had lost Gyarados, Salamence, and Naganadel.

The match was extremely close—neither side held a clear advantage.

Lance sent out his injured Hydreigon again, while Giovanni sent out Mega Kangaskhan.

Hydreigon did have aerial advantage, but unlike Naganadel, it wasn't a fast attacker, and Mega Kangaskhan had priority Sucker Punch.

With the speed of Sucker Punch closing the gap, Hydreigon struck back but was still defeated.

"That leaves you with only two Pokémon, Champion Lance."

With the advantage now his, even calm Giovanni couldn't help speaking.

"Feeling pleased just because you're temporarily ahead?" Lance sneered.

"You think you're better than me? Giovanni, let me tell you—today, you still can't beat me!"

"My dragon legion does not lose!"

He pulled out a luxury ball and threw it.

"Dragonite!"

A massive Dragonite landed heavily before him—a giant Dragonite.

"So it really is that one."

Giovanni narrowed his eyes slightly at its overwhelming presence.

Though Lance had swapped his primary lineup, he still brought the giant Dragonite.

Its huge body weakened its evasiveness, but its raw physique and power surpassed even Lance's ace Dragonite.

"Return, Kangaskhan."

Giovanni withdrew Mega Kangaskhan and sent out Great Tusk to take on the giant Dragonite.

The fight began. The giant Dragonite's power was overwhelming. Great Tusk fought hard but was eventually defeated—though not before landing a final Ice Spinner as a last strike.

Then Mega Kangaskhan returned for another intense clash.

Mega Kangaskhan had Ice Punch and Avalanche, but the giant Dragonite's power was terrifying. It countered Ice Punch with Fire Punch, matching it evenly.

Still, Giovanni found an opening—Avalanche landed cleanly, Ice Punch connected as well.

But the price was the loss of Mega Kangaskhan.

"I told you, Giovanni—you can't beat me!"

Lance's cold, confident voice carried across the field.

Now he held the advantage. Whoever led spoke first—fair enough.

"In your own words, Champion Lance… temporarily ahead, and already arrogant?"

Giovanni calmly retrieved Kangaskhan, adjusting his collar with effortless poise despite the tension.

"You…"

Lance glared hard at Giovanni, face emotionless.

"Then this will be the last."

Giovanni drew his final Poké Ball—the one that would decide victory or defeat.

"Landorus!"

With a low roar, a tiger-like figure descended before Giovanni—Landorus in its Therian Forme—its eyes sharp and imposing.

Lance studied it, brows tightening. Its aura alone made it clear—this Landorus was powerful.

Beep—Landorus (Therian Forme). Ground/Flying-type. Abundance Pokémon.

Ability: Intimidate

Moves: Sandsear Storm, Fissure, Outrage, Hammer Arm, Stone Edge…

Info: When it soars in its Therian Forme, it brings prosperity to the land, revered by people. One moment is enough for it to traverse the Hisui plains.

A legendary Incarnate!

So Giovanni truly had captured Landorus.

Many had guessed—but seeing it for real was different.

And if Giovanni saved it for last, it meant Landorus was even stronger than Mega Kangaskhan.

But it didn't end there—Giovanni took out a black Terastal Orb.

The orb flared with dazzling light.

Terastal!

A shining Therian Landorus emerged—its Tera Type: Flying.

Many spectators froze. Giovanni, a Ground specialist, using a Flying-Tera ace?

Unexpected.

The battle began. Under Intimidate, the giant Dragonite's Attack fell.

Landorus struck first with a sudden Slap—smacking Dragonite across the face.

Dragonite flinched. Stone Edge followed—jagged boulders exploding against its body.

When the dust cleared, the giant Dragonite had fallen.

Already worn down by Great Tusk and Mega Kangaskhan, it couldn't withstand a super-effective Stone Edge.

"Dragonite!"

Without wasting time, Lance sent out his final Pokémon—his ace Dragonite—and immediately Terastalized it.

A Tera Dragon Dragonite versus Tera Flying Landorus.

A dragon and a tiger faced off in the air, pressure crushing the stadium atmosphere.

Battle!

The two lunged.

Dragon didn't resist Flying—and that didn't matter. Neither side cared anymore about type charts.

Dragon Claw vs. Brick Break!

Breaking Swipe vs. Tera Blast!

Dragon Rush vs. Stone Edge!

The two clashed wildly in the sky, their wounds worsening.

But Lance's expression grew grim. Even with Tera Dragon, even with STAB bonuses, his Dragonite wasn't gaining ground.

Landorus only had Tera Blast and Fly as Flying moves—no others.

What did that mean?

Its power surpassed Dragonite's.

Even with non-STAB moves, it could suppress Dragonite.

The savage dragon and the descending land tiger collided yet again.

Dragon Rush vs. Fly!

Shockwaves tore through the air.

But after a brief struggle—

"Dragonite is failing…"

Spectators realized it at once. Dragon Rush's aura was being pushed back—Landorus was overpowering it.

Finally, Landorus shattered Dragon Rush's force and slammed into Dragonite, knocking it from the sky.

Giovanni commanded the follow-up: Landorus unleashed Outrage.

Lance's face darkened.

"Dragonite is unable to battle! Landorus wins!"

"Since Lance has lost all six Pokémon—Giovanni is victorious! Giovanni advances!"

The referee's voice boomed across the arena.

"Wooo—!"

Thunderous cheers erupted. To spectators, this full 6v6 battle had been incredible.

Giovanni stood on the field, savoring the moment.

"Looks like I've won."

He stepped toward Lance, wearing a faint smile.

"Even an ant crawling on the ground… can defeat the dragon soaring in the sky. Don't you think?"

"Hmph. This time, I lost."

Lance snorted coldly—not avoiding the truth.

He had given everything. No excuses.

But he was far from accepting defeat.

"Lance, I hope you remember…" Giovanni walked past him, one hand in his pocket.

"From now on—

you are the challenger."

(End of chapter)

---

---

---

# 505: Chapter 507: Ash, Why Aren’t You Competing in the World Championships?

"From now on, you are the challenger?"

Hearing Giovanni's parting words, a faint twitch appeared on Lance's face—but he quickly suppressed his emotions.Even so, his fists had unknowingly clenched tight.

He was frustrated—deeply unwilling to accept it.

But Lance understood well: no matter how he argued right now, it was meaningless.He lost.

Giovanni…!

Lance stared sharply at the middle-aged man's back like a drawn blade.It was fine—there would still be opportunities.

As one of the Top 8 from the ranking phase, he had one revival privilege.If he won the revival match, he could return.

Giovanni! Just wait. I'll be back!

The battle between Giovanni and Lance had ended—with the unexpected result of Giovanni "avenging" his old loss.

For Giovanni's supporters, it was a moment of pure triumph.

But for Lance's fans? Devastation.

No one expected that the Giovanni who had been defeated half a year ago would now overturn the heavens.

"That Landorus… was a bit too strong."

After watching the entire match, Lillie frowned slightly.

"Seriously. That was Lance's ace Dragonite, and it still got suppressed. Amazing!"Leon was equally astonished.

Lance's ace Dragonite was extremely powerful—yet Giovanni's Landorus, which he had only trained for half a year, fought the Tera Dragon Dragonite evenly in close combat, even slightly overpowering it.

"According to legend, Landorus appears to stop Thundurus and Tornadus when they fight. That might imply Landorus is stronger than both."Lillie mused.

Thundurus and Tornadus were known for their destructive, ill-natured personalities—bringing storms and ruining crops wherever they went. When they rampaged, Landorus always appeared to quell them.

Sound familiar?

Yes—almost like a Galar-version retelling of an ancient Hoenn myth.Of course, the "Forces of Nature" couldn't compare to the super-ancient Hoenn legends—the power gap was massive.

But Landorus was clearly stronger than the other two.With such base strength, and half a year of careful training, it wasn't strange that Landorus became Giovanni's new ace.

"Well, I'm looking forward to it. Facing Giovanni in battle will be spectacular."Leon said eagerly.

"Lance has fallen into the losers' bracket. I wonder if he'll manage to return later?"

"Maybe. It depends on the full Top 8 lineup."

Leon shook his head—no one knew yet.

After all, only two of the Top 8 had been decided so far.

With Dylan's group

"Congratulations, Uncle Giovanni! That was such a stylish victory."Selene beamed.

"Nothing surprising. I prepared a great deal for this day."

Giovanni chuckled—clearly in a good mood.

Of course he was.Defeating Lance on the world stage—nothing felt better.

With Beedrill banned, Giovanni had indeed worked extremely hard to defeat Lance.

"With this, one of ours is eliminated, and two advance."Leaf commented as she looked at Giovanni's portrait on the screen.Who would've believed it?

Last year this man was the Rocket Boss everyone hated—the source of Kanto's darkness.

And now, he had become one of the most famous and strongest trainers in Kanto.

Blue clicked his tongue unhappily, glancing at Red. If not for being matched against Red, he would've advanced for sure.

"The first two matchups were both Kanto trainers. Is the pairing system messing with us?" Leaf complained again.

Given they were at Indigo Plateau—the system was developed in Kanto—perhaps it really was biased.

"True strength doesn't worry about these things. Come on, let's get lunch. The rest are afternoon matches."Red said.

The Round of 16 usually took two days—four matches a day.

And now, with Red and Giovanni advancing, the remaining two matches of the day were in the afternoon.

Dylan's group headed to the athlete village.

With the official World Championships underway, the village had become lively, packed with tourists from all over the world, and lots of vendors.

They picked a restaurant with good reviews. On the way, they ran into many fans—after all, everyone in their group was highly recognizable.

Not long after they entered a private room, more people walked in—Oak Snowfield and the others.

"Hey hey—Ash! Misty! Brock! And Pikachu! Long time no see!"Dylan greeted them warmly.

It really had been a long time.

Ash had started in the Orange Islands this year, then moved to Johto for the Silver Conference.

So it had indeed been more than a year.

Dylan genuinely cared about the kid. They got along well, and he looked forward to the day when a fully grown Ash would challenge him.

"Long time no see!""Pika—chu!"

Dylan rubbed Pikachu's head—the same soft, familiar sensation.

Pikachu didn't resist; rather, it enjoyed it. Dylan's Viridian Force made most Pokémon feel naturally comfortable around him—unless they had terrible temperaments.

"Ash, how'd you do in the Silver Conference?" Dylan asked with interest.

This world wasn't the anime. Even without Dylan, events wouldn't match the original exactly.

"Uh… well…"Ash scratched his head awkwardly.

"Don't ask. He stopped at Top 16—even though Charizard tried so hard." Misty rolled her eyes.

"It wasn't my fault! They were all ridiculous! Poison this, Toxic Spikes that! Hex, Curse, Destiny Bond—come on!"Ash pouted, looking almost betrayed as he glanced at Dylan.

His tournament journeys these past two years had been rough.Last year, he was eliminated by Jeanette's poison team.This year?

He fought his way through chaos to reach Top 16—only to meet a slimy Ghost-type trainer.

His invincible Charizard was traded away by a Mismagius using Destiny Bond. His other Pokémon couldn't handle the opponent's ghostly tricks either.

And—no, he didn't meet Gary in Top 16.

Gary didn't compete in the Silver Conference this year—he attempted the World Championships instead.

And then…

He decided to switch to becoming a researcher.He felt maybe being a trainer wasn't really for him.

"…Ha."Dylan laughed awkwardly.

To be honest, the rise of Poison- and Ghost-type trainers in Kanto and Johto really was because of him.

Dylan quickly changed the subject.

"Say, Ash—why didn't you take part in the World Championships?"

"I did register! But…"

Ash's reply came naturally—he loved Pokémon battles, and the World Championships were exactly his kind of thing.

But then he hesitated.

"…I think I still want to travel more. Going on adventures with my partners—that's what I really want right now."

"I see."Dylan nodded.

"Traveling unfamiliar regions, meeting all sorts of Pokémon and people, hearing local legends, enriching your experiences—that truly is an excellent path for growth."

Giovanni sipped his tea, his deep voice rumbling calmly.

"Eh? Uh… yeah… Haha…"Ash blinked, confused, scratching his head.Seeing everyone look thoughtful, he could only laugh awkwardly.

Was that really what he meant?He just wanted adventure—like he'd dreamed of since he was a kid.

"No matter the reason, knowing what you want is the most important thing. Ash, don't mind what others say. Just follow your heart."Dylan smiled.

"Hehe—I know!" Ash grinned brightly.

"It's just… everyone is joining the World Championships, so not working on my ranking feels weird."

He sighed—this year the Championships were so huge that many people kept asking about his ranking.

"I once told Selene something—and I want to tell you the same thing."

Dylan glanced at Selene, who was devouring grilled meat like it was oxygen.He rolled his eyes.

Hearing her name, Selene instinctively looked up with her mouth full.

"All the sights you see, all the adventures you go through, all the encounters you experience—they'll become nourishment for your growth."

Dylan said warmly.

Selene blinked, recognizing the words.

"The World Championships is a place to test and prove yourself. But for you right now, Ash, it may not be the right path."

"Keep traveling. Follow your heart."

Dylan patted his shoulder.

"Since the moment you started your journey—don't forget its meaning."

"I believe that when you return from your travels, you'll be a very different trainer."

Yes—only through long, genuine travel would Ash become the strongest version of himself.It was his journey, his foundation.

"And still the same words, Ash—I'll be waiting for you to challenge me."

Dylan truly looked forward to the day when a fully grown Ash—using his strongest team—would challenge him.

A little transition here. As for the battle with a fully evolved Ash—it may be in the finale… or maybe not. Depends on how things go.

(End of chapter)

---

# 506: Chapter 508: How Will You Handle It?

"Welcome back to Indigo Arena! This morning we've already determined two of our Top Eight!"

"Now let's continue and see which trainers will advance next!"

The slick-back-haired announcer's passionate voice reignited the stadium atmosphere.

On the giant screen, portraits spun rapidly.

With Leaf and Giovanni advancing, only six matches remained. No one dared relax—on the contrary, tension was even higher.

After all, anyone who reached Top Sixteen had no weakness.

And most importantly…

"Why hasn't Champion Dylan appeared yet?"Selene kept complaining. Out of all contestants, Dylan was the one she cared about most.

This man was like a final boss just lurking among the remaining contestants, and she couldn't calm down at all.

What if someone got matched with him?!

"I really wish every match put Champion Dylan in the first slot so he can hurry up and advance!"

Hilda and the others laughed.

The first portrait stopped. A bright, smiling black-haired girl in a sunhat appeared.

Selene!

"Ha—finally my turn?!" Selene's big eyes lit up. After watching two intense battles that morning, she was bursting for action.

"Don't get too worked up. What if you get a tough matchup?" Leaf teased.

"Hmph! As long as it's not Dylan, anyone is fine!"Selene puffed up proudly, turning to Leaf.

"With my bond with Dylan, there's no way we'd meet this early! We'll fight in the finals! On the world's highest stage!"

That was her confidence.

She believed she'd be the one standing before Dylan in the end.

Red, Giovanni—anyone in the way would fall!

"Oh? Not necessarily. Look, you got matched with Dylan."Leaf grinned mischievously and gestured to the screen.

"What? Impossible! That must not happen!!"Selene froze in shock. She absolutely did not want a battle with Dylan in a "small place" like this.

She whipped her head toward the screen—then her cheeks puffed up angrily.

"Leaf!!"

"Hahaha—sorry, sorry, you're just too cute."Leaf laughed innocently.

"But your opponent is pretty special."

Selene raised an eyebrow—and saw the green-haired young man in a white cap.

The trainer chosen by the Dark Dragon of Ideals—N.

"Well—it's fine. I'll win!"

Flames lit in Selene's eyes.The legendary Zekrom, the Dragon of Ideals, huh?

A huge challenge—but…

He'd lost to Dylan before.

As Dylan's student, she couldn't embarrass him.

She looked toward Dylan. He smiled back.

"Good luck."

Selene's determination soared.

"Oh? That girl… she's frighteningly strong."Alder clicked his tongue as he looked at the brackets.

Selene—one of Kanto's "monsters," even defeating Champion Lance for his ranking.

She looked harmless and sunny, but she was undeniably one of the world's strongest trainers.

"N, what do you think?" Alder asked.

"Dylan's student, hmm… I'm looking forward to it."N's eyes shone gently.

Could Dylan's student withstand the lightning of the Dragon of Ideals?

…

"Alright! Both trainers are ready—please welcome them to the field!"

"Selene!"

"N!"

Thunderous cheers erupted.

From opposite tunnels, two figures emerged—the shorts-and-suspenders girl Selene, and the casually dressed N.

"Selene! You can do it!!"

"N will win! With the Dragon of Ideals, who besides Dylan can beat him?!"

"That's not true! Selene can!"

"Right! If defeating Zekrom is a miracle—Selene will make it happen!"

"Let's go!!"

The crowd roared.

Selene was one of Kanto's Elite Four and Dylan's former student, who had defeated Lance in the ranking phase.

N, though previously unknown, defeated Steven in Top 32—and most importantly, he used Zekrom.

One of the Unova Dragon pair of myth.Everyone knew Reshiram was with Dylan.Few knew where Zekrom was—until now.

"N, huh? Can that girl really win?" Lorelei murmured.

"Zekrom is extremely strong. Her odds aren't high," Bruno rumbled.

"In the wild, with her full team, maybe. But in a formal 1v1 format? Very hard," Agatha said calmly.

She understood that top champions could fight high legends, but only in unrestrained field battles.

"What do you think, Lance?" Lorelei asked.

After all, Lance had once held Zekrom.

He didn't answer.

Lorelei shrugged—he was cold-natured, and losing to Giovanni earlier couldn't help his mood.

"…Selene has the higher chance."

The sudden statement shocked them.

Lance believed Selene had better odds?

He ignored their expressions.Zekrom was strong—but never invincible.

If he fought it, he wasn't without chances.And Selene had defeated him.

Her arsenal of strategies was far deeper.

N was strong, but aside from Zekrom, Lance knew the rest of N's team lagged behind.That would be his fatal weakness.

And more importantly—the trainer's command mattered.

N commanding Zekrom… or Lance commanding Zekrom… who was stronger?

An obvious answer.

N simply couldn't pull out Zekrom's full power.

On the field—

The referee announced the rules, then backed away.

The pointer stopped—Selene had the first move.

She smiled, enlarging a Poké Ball.

"N."

"Yes, Selene?" N replied gently.

"Have you ever lost to anyone besides Dylan?"

The trainer chosen by Zekrom should have almost no defeats.

And N himself was strong.

"I have," N answered without hesitation.

Losing to Lance in the wild was good for him—it helped him face himself.

"I thought you never had." Selene blinked, almost disappointed.

"Is that a problem?" N asked.

"Nope! But I'm sorry—there's no way I can let you win today! I'll be sending you home here!"

Selene's energetic voice rang with blazing confidence.

She was determined to stand before Dylan.

"I see. Then show me."N smiled warmly.

The battle began.Selene opened with her powerhouse—Iron Valiant.

A brutally fast, brutally hard hitter.

N sent out Klinklang—resistant to Fairy, weak to Fighting.

There was no suspense—Iron Valiant was too fast.

Archeops fell next.

Cetitan—down.

Carracosta—Iron Valiant barely won.

Selene switched to Gengar—Mega Evolution.

N responded with Tera Dark Zoroark, but it fell as well.

Five Pokémon defeated.

"You're incredible, Selene."N looked at her in admiration.She was far more terrifying than Hilda and Mei.

Selene grinned happily.

"Then let me show you—my final support!"

"My sixth Pokémon—Zekrom!"

A deep dragon's roar shook the field as the black dragon descended heavily before N, its gaze full of overwhelming might.

"Zekrom is my last pillar," N said softly. "If you want to move forward—you must defeat it."

He watched the burning determination in Selene's eyes.

"Come—show me, Dylan's disciple.How will you face Zekrom?"

(End of chapter)

Merry Christmas

---

# 507: Chapter 509: What an Interesting Battle!

"Zekrom, huh~! So cool."

Selene's big eyes glittered as she stared at the overwhelmingly oppressive Zekrom. Although she took part in the Suou Island battle, by the time she and Leaf and Blue arrived, Zekrom never actually appeared—it had stayed hidden in the storm clouds.

And after Lance lost, Zekrom left on its own.

So this was the first time Selene had ever seen Zekrom with her own eyes—especially appearing on the battlefield as her opponent.

As for how to deal with Zekrom…

Tactics flashed through her mind; she had already planned everything before the battle began.

"Zekrom's power is ridiculous, but Selene's Pokémon are still in great condition."

Alder still felt uneasy. From some of Selene's earlier battles, it was clear—

This girl's tactics were insanely unpredictable. She didn't fixate on beating opponents head-on.

"Trust N. Zekrom isn't something that can be taken down so easily."

Iris, on the other hand, had complete confidence in N and Zekrom—after all, it was Unova's last remaining dragon god!

On the field—

"Zekrom."

N called softly.

Zekrom's intimidating golden eyes locked onto Mega Gengar. Its massive body rose slowly, gold lightning crawling across its obsidian armor.

"Thunder!"

BOOM!!

A massive golden bolt crashed down toward Mega Gengar.

This was just a probe—N had seen some of Selene's matches. She looked harmless, but when she fought, she could be downright sinister.

And her Mega Gengar wasn't something to take lightly.

Mega Gengar quickly slipped underground, dodging the attack.

The thunder crashed into the arena, blowing sparks through the air.

"Gengar, Curse!" Selene called without hesitation.

Mega Gengar rose from a corner of the arena. A decayed gray nail materialized before it.

"Thunder!"

N's gaze sharpened.

Zekrom dove, electricity screaming through the air again.

Will it make it in time?

Everyone stared—could Zekrom stop the Curse?

If Curse landed on Zekrom, N would be doomed!

But at that moment, Selene suddenly smiled sweetly.

N caught that expression—and felt a terrible premonition.

"Hehehe~ got you. Gengar—Destiny Bond!"

"Geh-he-he!"

The gray nail vanished. Mega Gengar's eyes flared with eerie light.

Destiny Bond?!

N's pupils shrank. Not good!

"Zekrom!"

Zekrom tried to redirect the Thunder, but it was too late.

BOOM!!

The devastating bolt struck Mega Gengar. Thunder exploded outward, wind and static tearing through the field.

"Nice one, Selene! Mega Gengar won't tank that!"

Leaf brightened. Mega Gengar had already taken damage, and its defense wasn't great. Even without a type advantage, Thunder from Zekrom hurt like crazy.

Destiny Bond would activate!

"It's clever bait, but it might not take Zekrom down outright," Giovanni murmured.

"Huh?" Blue blinked. Destiny Bond should have forced a trade.

Could Zekrom really withstand it?

"Indeed. Legendary Pokémon have far higher resilience—especially a legend like Zekrom," Dylan said.

Reality wasn't like a game—moves weren't guaranteed to activate perfectly. Just like how Protect wasn't guaranteed in the anime, and a random Clefairy couldn't magically ignore a Rayquaza's Draco Meteor.

Likewise, you couldn't expect Curse or Destiny Bond to instantly drop a high-tier legend.

With Zekrom's legendary physiology and strength, bringing it down with Destiny Bond wasn't impossible—but Selene's Mega Gengar pulling it off? The odds weren't high.

"But Selene's Mega Gengar is strong. It's not impossible," Dylan added.

The dust cleared.

Mega Gengar lay fainted, no longer Mega-Evolved.

A gray aura drifted out of its body, shooting straight into Zekrom.

Gray light flashed on Zekrom's armor—its body shuddered violently, hunched over as if in painful resistance.

"Zekrom!"

N's expression changed.

Selene recalled Gengar, smiling in satisfaction.

So what if it was Zekrom? As Dylan's disciple, she could handle this—piece of cake!

But soon the effect wore off. Zekrom finally steadied itself, though clearly in terrible shape.

"Huh???"

Selene's eyes widened.

It didn't fall?!

Destiny Bond couldn't drop it? Was Zekrom cheating?!

The crowd erupted in shock.

"Zekrom, are you alright?" N asked, worried.

"I'll manage… barely."

Zekrom's voice was low and strained—clearly Destiny Bond had hit hard.

N still looked tense, but his gaze toward Selene changed.

This girl…

"Tch—legends really are something. But if Destiny Bond didn't work, I'll just try something else."

Selene didn't mind. Zekrom was badly hurt—that was proof Destiny Bond did something.

She pulled out another Poké Ball.

"Weezing!"

A Weezing floated out before her.

Weezing wasn't one of Selene's main lineup, but she liked using it occasionally.

It simply knew way too many useful tricks.

"Weezing… if I recall, it can also learn Destiny Bond."

N's face darkened.

With Zekrom this injured… another Destiny Bond could finish it.

"Weezing, Toxic!"

While N wavered, Selene didn't hesitate.

Weezing lunged straight at the legendary dragon, spitting a spray of deep purple venom.

Poison-type Toxic!

N inhaled sharply.

Damn it!

Curse, Destiny Bond, Toxic—

Could she use ANYTHING normal?!

"Thunder!"

Zekrom blasted the Toxic apart—and the bolt slammed into Weezing.

When Weezing floated up again, it was in awful condition.

"Weezing!"

Selene called again, and Weezing darted forward.

N's expression twisted. If he used Thunder again, he could definitely knock Weezing out—but what if it used Destiny Bond?!

But with Weezing already inches from Zekrom—

N made his decision.

"Zekrom! Thunder!"

Zekrom rose slightly. A golden bolt shot down.

N stared at Weezing—

"Destiny Bond!" Selene shouted.

As expected!

"Zekrom!"

But this time N was prepared.

Zekrom growled, shifting the lightning so it grazed past Weezing—avoiding the trigger window. The bolt flashed dangerously close.

"Selene, you're underestimating me."

N said lowly.

Destiny Bond couldn't be used consecutively.

Now was the perfect time to finish Weezing.

"Zekrom, Thunder!"

Lightning surged.

"Nope—totally not underestimating you."

Selene beamed.

"I wasn't planning to use Destiny Bond this time."

"Weezing! Explosion!"

N's eyes widened.

Weezing's face twisted into a crazed grin as its entire body ignited with blinding white light.

It became a miniature sun—

"Zekrom!!"

Zekrom tried to flee, but—

BOOOOOOM!!!

The sun detonated. A violent shockwave tore outward, shaking the stadium. The flash was so bright the audience couldn't open their eyes.

A mushroom cloud billowed upward.

Explosion.

"How is this happening?!"

N shielded his face. His blood ran cold.

A blow this huge—even a full-strength Zekrom would feel it.

But right now…

Could Zekrom withstand it?

He already suspected the answer.

"Insane! That Explosion was perfect!!"

Blue gasped.

"If Thunder hit—Destiny Bond. If Thunder avoided—Explosion. Selene thought of everything," Giovanni praised.

Time felt painfully slow.

Finally—

With a heavy crash, Zekrom fell from the sky, battered beyond recognition.

It heaved breath after breath, glaring weakly at the unconscious Weezing.

That thing!!

"It's still standing?!"

Selene recalled Weezing, stunned.

"Guess I'll need… another explosion. Not bad for the Dragon of Ideals~"

She grabbed another Poké Ball.

Zekrom, still with sharp hearing despite the pain, heard her…

…and immediately felt dizzy.

Thud—

"Zekrom and Weezing are both unable to battle!"

The referee peeked nervously—Zekrom wasn't showing the usual swirly eyes but… it was clearly out cold.

He absolutely did NOT dare poke Zekrom to check.

"All six of N's Pokémon are unable to battle. Selene wins—Selene advances!"

Cheers erupted.

"I win! What a fun match!"

Selene flashed a big bright grin at the cameras, making a playful victory sign.

Compared to her, N stared silently at Zekrom… then even more silently at Selene.

Fun?

Battles between trainers always offered something to learn.

So what did he gain from this?

Curse.

Destiny Bond.

Toxic.

Explosion…

N stared blankly.

He didn't feel the "fun" at all.

(End of chapter)
