# 508: Chapter 510: Kyogre, Bless Me!

"I can't believe it! Selene has defeated the legendary Zekrom! She advances to the Top 8!"

The slick-back–haired announcer's excited shout boomed across the entire stadium.

Of course he was thrilled—he was from Kanto. And although he had to maintain neutrality as a professional host, seeing a Kanto Elite Four member advance in such dominant fashion naturally made him proud!

The crowd erupted.

"ELITE SELENE!!! (voice crack)"

"I knew it! How could one Zekrom just run wild? Kanto's Elite Four is built DIFFERENT!"

"Too dirty! Destiny Bond into Explosion? Shameless!"

"You joking? Smart tactics suddenly become 'shameless' to you?"

"Forget 'clean' or not. Did she beat your unbeatable Zekrom or not? And with just TWO main battlers."

The internet went wild as well.

Especially Unova netizens—every last one of them felt stifled and bitter.

Zekrom's strength wasn't in question. One Thunder took out the weakened Mega Gengar, another nearly knocked out Weezing in one shot.

Zekrom was monstrously strong.

But instead of losing through sheer power, it got outplayed.

To them, that was infuriating.

"So strong~!"

Hilda looked at the girl on the field and sighed in admiration.

She and the other top Unova trainers knew exactly how strong Zekrom was.

And yet Selene had handled it with only two Pokémon.

"Indeed. As expected of Dylan's student."

Alder nodded, convinced.

"But I suppose this is good for Rosa. One less terrifying opponent for her."

Unova now only had Rosa left—N was out.

Although… the remaining contestants were all monsters anyway, so it wasn't exactly comforting.

On the field, Selene and the thoughtful-looking N exchanged a few polite words before heading off.

Next was the final match of the day.

"There are ten contestants left. The chance of drawing Dylan is one in nine…"

Leon rubbed his cheek. He absolutely did NOT want to face Dylan right now.

If Dylan dared to bring Primal Kyogre in his very first match… who knew what he'd throw out next?!

"Come on, big bro's luck is always good! No way you'll get that unlucky~"

Hop grinned, hands behind his head.

"Besides Dylan, big bro can beat anyone!"

He adored Leon—Leon was his idol and lifelong goal.

"That's not true, Hop. I'm not scared of Mr. Leon anymore."

Chloe tilted her head with a smile.

She lost to Leon at the end of last year, but now she believed she was fully qualified to fight him again—

And to win.

"Yes, even without Dylan, we can't get careless. We must give everything."

Leon laughed, looking at Chloe with fiery enthusiasm.

This was why he loved the World Championship!

Trainers from all over the world gathering and fighting their hardest for the title of strongest—

Nothing was more exhilarating.

Battle! Yes!!

Half an hour later, the giant screen spun through the final contestants.

Everyone looked up nervously.

Soon, the first portrait stopped.

A cute girl appeared on the screen, wearing a bow-like head accessory—bright and cheerful.

May.

"Huh? My turn?"

May's eyes sparkled as she stared at the still-spinning second portrait.

Steven watched nervously—

Please don't be Dylan! Please!

"Kyogre bless us! Kyogre bless us! Don't match May with Dylan, please please please!"

Wallace clasped his hands, praying dramatically.

Big ol' pancake-fish, do SOMETHING! You're Hoenn's legend—protect us Hoenn folk!!

Under Wallace's "prayer," the second portrait slowed…

and revealed a chic girl wearing a red hat, with ash-blonde wavy hair—

Kalos Champion Serena.

May vs. Serena!

"Whew… Kalos's champion. May, do your best."

Wallace patted his chest in relief and encouraged her.

"Mhm~ don't worry, Wallace!"

May smiled brightly, eyes curving.

"Victory never slips past my fingers. Watch me."

Across the stadium, Serena studied May's portrait.

Hoenn's "monster," huh?

"Be careful, that girl is the one who caught Latios," Diantha reminded softly.

May wasn't Hoenn's champion—

but everyone knew it was only a matter of time.

Currently, Hoenn's strongest was undeniably May.

"I know. I'll treat this seriously—and I will win."

Serena nodded firmly.

"I'm not who I used to be. I'll win. I'll keep winning. Until…"

She pressed her lips together, straightened her clothes, and walked toward the battle entrance.

"Serena…"

Diantha watched her go, expression heavy.

"This should be interesting."

Dylan raised an eyebrow, intrigued.

The protagonist of Emerald vs. the protagonist of XY.

A main-character showdown.

Who would win?

"Alright! Both contestants are ready! Let's welcome them to the field!"

The announcer's enthusiastic voice echoed.

"May!"

"Serena!"

The two girls stepped out from opposite entrances.

"Whoa—them both being this cute is illegal. Who do we even cheer for?"

"May all the way!! Hoenn's last hope!"

"Shut up! Serena is Kalos's CHAMPION. What's May?"

"LMAO, Kalos champ has no weight. May's a Master Rank No.7. Your Serena is what, No. what exactly?"

"Oh-hoh! FIGHT! I want chaos!!"

The stadium atmosphere was lively and positive,

but online?

Hoenn and Kalos fans were tearing each other apart.

Both regions had only one contestant left—

they were desperate to see their favorite reach the Top 8.

May and Serena, unaware of the online war, stood in their positions.

The referee announced the rules.

The first-move indicator stopped on Serena's side.

Serena drew her Poké Ball. Her expression turned serious.

"Sorry, but I'm not handing you this win."

Her voice was calm, disciplined—Serena had always been this type.

She worked so hard to become Kalos champion. She fought so hard to reach this point.

Only one step away from the Top 8—

She would not fall here.

"Of course. We'll fight with everything we've got—I want to advance too!"

May blinked in surprise, then smiled.

Her eyes sharpened.

For herself…

For Steven and Wallace…

For her parents watching proudly…

She could not lose.

But she knew—this would be tough.

Serena was one of those trainers who hovered between Master Rank and Ultra Rank late in the season.

She didn't hit Master Rank during placement,

but she was still extremely strong.

"Alright."

Serena didn't speak further. There was no need.

Battles spoke best.

She removed her pink hat and tied up her wavy hair—a gesture she always made before serious battles.

"Meowstic, let's go!"

Her female Meowstic landed gracefully before her, staring coldly at May.

"A Psychic-type Meowstic, huh?"

May considered briefly, then threw out her own Poké Ball.

"Aggron!"

The ferocious Aggron slammed onto the field, growling at Meowstic.

Meowstic narrowed its feline eyes, unfazed.

"A battle between two monsters… this is gonna be intense."

Nemona squeezed Penny's hand, practically shaking in excitement.

She'd been eliminated by Serena earlier, but she treasured thrilling fights more than rankings—Serena had given her an amazing match.

Now Serena vs. May?

She could barely sit still.

"Mm. I really don't know who'll win."

Penny agreed, watching the two Pokémon closely.

"Ever since Blue defeated her, Serena has been training nonstop—really pushing herself."

Leaf tilted her head.

"Yes, and the results show. Her team is much stronger now."

Penny nodded. Had she not improved, there was no guarantee she would've beaten Nemona in the Top 32.

Nemona was also a monster.

(End of chapter)

---

---

---

# 509: Chapter 511: I Knew You’d Do It!

"Brother, who do you think will win?"

Lillie looked curiously at the two people in the arena and asked Dylan.

"Who knows."

"Who knows."

Two overlapping voices rang out. Dylan glanced at Leaf, who was rolling her eyes.

"Knew you'd answer like that. Lillie, you still don't get it? Dylan's always like this."

Leaf made a cute face at Dylan, as if saying she'd already seen through him completely.

"No wonder you understand me so well," Dylan chuckled.

"But honestly, I really can't predict who'll win. There are too many variables in a battle, and their strength isn't far apart."

He shrugged.

If it were purely based on their original game versions, he would naturally lean toward May. Even if they were both protagonists, some protagonists simply had more going for them.

The strongest, without question, were Rei and Akari from Legends: Arceus — the "Hisui superhumans" blessed by Arceus itself. Being handpicked by the Alpha Pokémon was practically like being its "illegitimate children." They were on a completely different level.

Next were Elio and Selene from Ultra Sun & Moon. Selene's achievements were outright terrifying.

Before even becoming Champion, she managed to defeat Necrozma three times — including Ultra Necrozma — eventually subduing it. After becoming Champion, she single-handedly crushed the entire Rainbow Rocket organization, beating Giovanni's entire multiverse ensemble by herself.

No other protagonist even came close.

Honestly, even without Dylan in this world, Rainbow Rocket would never have succeeded. Once Selene and the others grew up, Giovanni would've been taught a painful lesson anyway.

As for Serena and May, Dylan personally leaned toward May. But this wasn't the original plotline, and this Serena had strengthened her entire lineup.

Back then, Serena's team really wasn't so strong.

Serena and May's battle began, and as expected of the "monsters" of their respective regions, both girls fought at an exceptional level.

And both had captured a legendary Pokémon — May's Latios, Serena's Volcanion.

The battle raged on for quite a while, and both sides fought fiercely.

But battles must end eventually. Volcanion fell to Latios's Luster Purge. Serena sent out her final trump card — Pheromosa.

Pheromosa struck first and took down Latios. But before fainting, Latios set up Rain Dance.

May sent out her ace, Swampert — and Mega-Evolved immediately.

Under Mega Evolution, Swampert's body enlarged significantly, its muscles surging with overwhelming strength. One look was enough to know how terrifying a single punch would be.

—Beep!

Swampert (Mega Evolution), Water/Ground type, Mud Fish Pokémon

Ability: Swift Swim

Moves: Earthquake, Hammer Arm, Liquidation, High Horsepower, Ice Punch…

Description: Its upper body muscles are extremely developed. Mega Evolution reinforces its aquatic power, letting it unleash tremendous strength both in water and on land.

"This Rain Dance was way too clutch."

Seeing Latios leave Rain Dance behind before fainting made Wallace tense up.

Without rain, Mega Swampert would've struggled against Pheromosa. Pheromosa's speed was absurd. But in rain, Swift Swim doubled Mega Swampert's speed.

Even if it still wasn't quite as fast as Pheromosa, at least it wouldn't be toyed with helplessly.

And with Mega Swampert's power, landing even one hit — even a neutral one — would severely injure Pheromosa.

Ultra Beasts were extreme, and Pheromosa's defenses were paper-thin.

"Yes, May has a chance."

Steven nodded, eyes locked on the battlefield.

In the arena, Terastallized Fighting-type Pheromosa stood tall and proud, its cold gaze on Mega Swampert.

Mega Swampert planted its massive arms into the ground, staring back fiercely.

This was the final clash.

"I won't lose! Not here — never here!"

Serena's eyes hardened. She hadn't expected May to be this difficult. She'd given everything, yet the fight still reached this point.

They were both down to their last Pokémon. This was it.

"As long as I beat Pheromosa — I win!"

May inhaled deeply to steady herself. She needed full focus.

She refused to lose — not with so many people cheering for her, not with her Pokémon fighting their hearts out, not with Roy and Wallace supporting her, not with her parents watching.

She couldn't fall here.

May met Serena's gaze. Both saw the same unshakeable resolve in the other's eyes.

Battle!

Almost simultaneously, under the pouring rain, the Tera-Pheromosa and Mega Swampert launched their charge.

Mega Swampert roared, tightening its right fist and swinging with devastating force.

Pheromosa slipped past the blow with a simple sidestep, spinning into a whip-like kick.

Mega Swampert raised its left arm and blocked it, the massive impact only making its frame tremble before stabilizing again.

Such brute force was terrifying.

Pheromosa narrowed its eyes, leaping back to avoid another punch.

But Mega Swampert refused to let up. In rain, it moved far faster than usual and pounced again.

Those huge fists were bigger than Pheromosa's entire torso. A single clean hit would be catastrophic.

The pace was intense. Mega Swampert's attacks poured out relentlessly. Pheromosa dodged again and again, countering whenever it could.

But every counter was blocked by Swampert's arm-shields.

"Swift Swim…"

Seeing Mega Swampert's brutal speed, Serena's brows knitted tightly.

Pheromosa could still keep up — barely — but everyone knew the saying: a long defense is bound to crumble.

Eventually, she would slip.

"If she can just outlast the rain, she'll have a chance!"

In the stands, Diantha was tense. Rain boosted Mega Swampert tremendously. Once rain ended, its speed would drop, giving Pheromosa space to work.

Could Pheromosa hold out long enough?

She didn't know — but that was the only hope.

Reality, however, was cruel.

Pheromosa couldn't manage it. After narrowly dodging High Horsepower, it failed to evade the sweeping Hammer Arm.

The blow sent Pheromosa flying. It almost couldn't get back up.

"It's basically over."

Dylan shook his head. Pheromosa wasn't knocked out immediately, but that Hammer Arm nearly emptied its HP bar.

From here, turning the tables was almost impossible.

To win, Pheromosa needed to attack — but attacking meant taking hits. Mega Swampert could simply tank one and trade back for a decisive blow.

And that was exactly what happened. When the rain stopped and Mega Swampert slowed down, Pheromosa landed several chip attacks, none decisive.

Then Mega Swampert finally caught it — with Blizzard.

Pheromosa couldn't take it.

In the end, Mega Swampert narrowly defeated Serena's Tera Pheromosa.

"Pheromosa is unable to battle. Mega Swampert wins."

"Serena has lost all six Pokémon. May is the winner and advances."

Serena's face went pale. She stared at Pheromosa, stunned.

She had lost.

"We have a winner! After an intense battle, May defeats Serena and advances to the quarterfinals!"

The host's passionate voice filled the arena.

Cries rang out everywhere — joy, regret, excitement, disappointment.

"Waaaaah…"

"Hahahaha! I knew May would win!"

"Way to go, May! We knew you were the best!"

"What a great battle. But what a shame… she was so close."

"I really feel for Serena. She tried so hard."

The audience — especially those in Hoenn — was thrilled.

"Haah! You did amazing, Swampert!"

May rushed to Swampert, healed its wounds, and patted its shoulder with delight.

Swampert growled softly, its fierce eyes turning gentle. It was proud of helping its trainer win.

After chatting briefly with it, May looked toward Serena.

She could feel how down Serena was. Anyone would be. Wanting to win so badly, giving everything — only to fall short.

May understood. She would've felt the same.

Serena quietly removed her headband and looked at the Poké Ball in her hand for a long time before letting out a weak smile.

"That was a great battle."

A clear voice reached her ears.

Serena looked up and saw her opponent.

"Congratulations on advancing."

She steadied herself and shook May's hand with a strained smile.

"Thanks. It really wasn't easy. You're strong, Serena."

"And yet I still lost." Serena sighed.

"But no use dwelling on it. May, I hope the rest of your matches go smoothly."

She forced herself to perk up, offering May a warm smile. She knew exactly why May had come over.

Even though she was upset, after a moment to breathe, she'd already regained her footing.

"After reaching the quarterfinals, the pressure only gets worse."

Serena warned her. Compared to the round of 16, the top 8 were monsters among monsters.

"Ah— I'll try my best! I'm not expecting to be champion. If I reach the semifinals, that's a win already!"

May pumped her fists.

She'd set a small goal:

No need to aim for the championship.

Make it to the top 4 — stabilize, then fight for top 2!

(End of Chapter)

---

---

---

# 510: Chapter 512: Leon vs. Dylan?

The first day's round of 16 had ended, and the four trainers who advanced were already the center of heated discussion.

And once again, Kanto dominated the conversation.

Why?

Because among the four who had advanced — aside from May — every single one was from Kanto.

(What else could "strongest region" possibly mean.jpg)

Kanto fans straightened their backs with pride. And with Dylan and Leaf still unmatched, if everything lined up perfectly, Kanto could send five trainers into the top 8!

If that really happened, it'd practically be a national holiday for Kanto citizens.

And at this point, Dylan was considered guaranteed to advance — none of the remaining eight could stop him.

So now, for Kanto fans, the real question was whether Leaf could hold her own.

Hilda had plenty of thoughts about that.

The next day arrived quickly, and the competition resumed.

"Among the eight remaining, as long as we don't hit Dylan, and we don't have to fight each other, we all still have a good chance of breaking in together."

Leon looked at the eight portraits on the big screen.

Dylan, Leaf, Leon, Gloria, Selene, Cynthia, Dawn, Blue.

"Mhm!" Gloria nodded vigorously — she thought so too.

As the host announced the draw, the portraits began spinning rapidly.

Soon, the first portrait stopped — a blue-haired girl in a white wool cap.

Dawn of Sinnoh!

"Eh? It's me?" Dawn brightened immediately, delighted to see her face appear.

"I hope you match with someone suitable," Cynthia said with a warm smile.

Moments later, Dawn's opponent flashed onto the screen — and she froze.

Shining blonde hair, elegant pale face —It was Cynthia.

Dawn vs. Cynthia.

A Sinnoh civil war.

"W-what??" Dawn was stunned.

"So our battle begins here, Dawn."

Cynthia raised her brows a little, but then smiled and turned to her young challenger.

"Cynthia…!"

"Don't hesitate, Dawn. You want to become Sinnoh Champion, don't you? Then on this grand stage, show me everything you have."

Cynthia swept her long golden hair back, her voice gentle.

No matter what, a battle against Dawn was inevitable. If fate arranged it for today, all the better.

"Yes! I understand!"

Dawn's eyes flared with determination.

Becoming a champion as strong and graceful as Cynthia — that was her dream. And today was the greatest chance she could ask for.

Facing Cynthia on such a stage was enough to ignite her blood.

That's the gaze I wanted to see. Dawn… I believe you'll surpass me one day.

Seeing the fierce resolve in Dawn's eyes, Cynthia couldn't help but smile deeply.

Since accepting Dawn's challenge last year, she had known — this girl's talent far exceeded her own.

Dawn was a natural-born monster.

But to reach the top of Sinnoh, she would still have to topple its highest peak.

Cynthia — Sinnoh's strongest Champion.

"Now, let's welcome both trainers!"

The announcer roared.

"Dawn!"

"Cynthia!"

Thunderous applause filled the arena as the two silhouettes emerged.

"Damn it! Why did Dawn have to run into Cynthia? That's so unlucky!"

"Look at it another way — at least one of them is guaranteed a place in the top 8. Depends on whether Cynthia can withstand Dawn's impact…"

"I still don't think Dawn can beat Cynthia."

"Where does your confidence come from? Even Leon can't be sure of beating Selene right now. Unless Sinnoh's 'monster' isn't a real monster after all."

"These new-era monsters are insane. I honestly think Cynthia isn't Dawn's match anymore."

Excitement filled both the arena and the livestream chat.

Cynthia's popularity was among the highest of any Champion — beauty, grace, personality — she was flawless.

Her strength was undisputed as well. Although the rise of the "monsters" knocked her out of the Master Class, she still held a stable spot within the top 20.

Unlike a certain Water-type Champion, who was about to slip into the thirties…

The rules were announced, the coin toss spun — and landed on Cynthia.

She would move first.

"What a pity."

"What are you pitying?" Leaf eyed Dylan suspiciously.

"I kinda wanted to fight Cynthia."

Dylan sighed wistfully. As Sinnoh's Champion, she deserved to witness Sinnoh's "High Deity" in person — meaning Arceus.

He had been planning to give someone the honor of facing Arceus directly… but now Cynthia wouldn't be that someone.

"You like Cynthia that much? You want to eliminate her personally?" Leaf blinked. Lillie also glanced over.

"What? No. It's just—you know. I don't know who to use it on."

Dylan looked genuinely troubled. Arceus had agreed to take the field for him once.

A blessing beyond words.

But deciding who would experience Arceus' divine smiting?That was the problem.

"…"Leaf's eyelid twitched wildly.

Cynthia hadn't offended him in the least — they got along well! And he still wanted to hit her with Arceus?

"Who do you think will win, Dylan?"

Leaf ignored that entire disaster of a topic. Who Dylan planned to smite with Arceus was none of her business.

Whoever that "lucky" victim was, their expression would definitely be unforgettable. Honestly, Leaf kind of wanted to see it.

"How do I see it? With my eyes." Dylan shrugged.

"Cynthia… probably isn't Dawn's match," Lillie said hesitantly.

"Not 'probably.' Definitely. Cynthia isn't her match." Leaf shook her head.

"Cynthia's strong, and she's improved a lot. But it's not enough," Dylan added.

And sure enough, the outcome required no explanation. Cynthia couldn't stop the monster that was Dawn.

Her ace, Mega Garchomp, finally fell to Dawn's Water Tera Empoleon.

Dawn advanced.

Cynthia accepted the result without bitterness — instead, she was happy.

Happy that Dawn had surpassed her.

The fifth slot in the top eight was set.

After a short break, the sixth match was drawn.

Only six trainers remained — and among them lurked a final boss.

The screen froze.

Leaf vs. Rosa!

"Eh? Another Unova challenger?" Leaf blinked. Rosa, like Hilda, was from Unova too.

"Great!! Perfect!!"

On the other side, Rosa practically bounced with excitement.

"Hilda, sit tight and watch. I'll avenge you today! How dare she eliminate you just because she's cute?!"

"Hmph! Even heaven wants me to win!"

Rosa stood tall, fiery with confidence.

"…I lost because I wasn't good enough. What are you even talking about?" Hilda stared at the dramatic, chuunibyou bun-headed girl.

"And don't underestimate Rosa. Her Pokémon… feel weird. Or maybe she does."

Hilda rubbed her brows. She wasn't sure how to phrase it — something about Rosa was off.

"Indeed. Be cautious. That child defeated Hilda," Alder warned.

"Hmph! Relax! I never underestimate my opponents. But I believe in myself — I won't lose to a little kid like Hilda did."

Rosa waved dismissively.

She was Rosa! Unova's representative! Other than Dylan, who could beat her?

No one! Obviously no one!

She was unstoppable in Unova!

"You just keep bragging. You're gonna eat those words later," Hilda muttered.

"Don't make me laugh, Hilda. That girl, Leaf or whatever — I'll crush her!"

"Top 8! I'm coming!"

Then—

"This is impossible!! Why is she that strong?!"

Rosa stared blankly at her collapsed Serperior… and then at the adorable little Pichu covered in tiny red flowers.

Snake eats mouse — that was the rule, wasn't it?!

And what was with that Volt Tackle? Why was it so strong?Where was she borrowing that power from?!

Rosa felt dizzy. Her worldview cracked.

"Serperior is unable to battle. Pichu wins!"

"All six of Rosa's Pokémon are down. Leaf is the winner and advances!"

The referee declared the result mercilessly.

Leaf advanced — overjoyed.

Rosa was eliminated — heartbroken — especially remembering all her bold declarations beforehand.

It hurt. A lot.

Then came the afternoon match.

Ever since noon, Leon felt strangely uneasy — his eyelids kept twitching nonstop.

Soon, he found out why.

"I KNEW it. Only four of us left — of course it'd be me."

Leon smacked his face with a loud slap, unable to look at the screen.

Two portraits appeared:

Leon on the left.Dylan on the right.

Leon vs. Dylan.

Leon felt absolute despair.

There were fifteen possible opponents.Why… WHY him?

He worked so hard for Galar, lived with integrity, had no vices — well, unless loving battles counted.

Wait…

Suddenly, he remembered.

Last time they fought, Dylan had told him:

Next time, he wouldn't use any broken dragon-type legends.

(End of Chapter)

---

# 511: Chapter 513: I, Leon, Have Seen Far Bigger Things!

"Now, let's welcome our two competitors to the field!"

The slick-backed host shouted with a voice louder than ever, practically pouring every ounce of strength into the announcement.

"Dylan!!"

"Leon!!"

"WOO––!!"

Amid deafening screams, two silhouettes slowly emerged from the opposite player tunnels.

"AAAAAH! Champion Dylan!!"

"The unbeatable Champion!"

"Show us what true Dark-type supremacy looks like!"

"Why is it Leon again… he really doesn't deserve this..."

"Finally!! Hahaha—seeing Champion Dylan battle in person!"

Without a doubt, the entire Indigo Arena had exploded with energy. The overwhelming hype had ignited the entire venue until it was boiling over.

The strongest Champion! The undisputed world No. 1!

"Holy—this is insane! What is happening?!" Misty's ears were ringing from the sudden eruption of screams. She stared blankly at the crowd, faces flushed red as they shouted their lungs out.

It took several full minutes before the arena finally calmed down to a manageable level.

"Dylan's popularity is just that high. Universally acknowledged as No. 1—no one can question it," Professor Oak muttered while rubbing his temples. He was getting old, and this noise level was starting to become lethal.

Still, he wasn't surprised at all.

"But last time it wasn't this exaggerated," Gary said, puzzled.

"That was different. Last time he fought Wallace. This time it's Leon." Oak shook his head.

"Wallace's popularity can't compare to Leon's. And the first battle that truly made Dylan's name echo across the world was against Leon."

"Even now, that match is still talked about. In sheer spectacle, nothing can surpass it. Those Dark-type legends aren't something anyone gets to see."

"Though for poor Leon, the experience probably wasn't great."

Oak chuckled. He personally loved seeing those mythical beasts fight.

But if you stood where Leon had stood… it was probably pure suffering. Absolutely no battle experience—just overwhelming pressure.

Of course, the audience didn't care. All they wanted was excitement, and seeing legendary Pokémon unleash their power right in front of them was more than enough.

"And today, it's Dylan vs. Leon again. What do you think Dylan will use this time?"

Oak stroked his chin with interest.

From both a viewer and professional standpoint, he desperately hoped Dylan would bring more rare legendary Pokémon to fight Leon.

"Hmmmm…"

Misty and the others exchanged glances, their eyes shining dangerously.

They sincerely wished Leon the best…

But also hoped he suffered just a little.

On the field—

Dylan appeared in his signature black outfit: a fitted shirt and long black slacks — simple, casual, yet striking.

But what truly stood out was that calm, extraordinary aura he carried.

The referee announced the rules, bowed to both, and slowly stepped out of the battlefield.

"Looks like fate really wants us to meet, Leon."

Dylan waved cheerfully at Leon.

"I'd rather fate gave me a break," Leon sighed. He didn't mind facing Dylan later in the tournament — but round of 16? Way too early.

Sure, he had his one revival right… but that wasn't a guaranteed safety net.

"Don't be like that, Leon. It's not like I arranged it — this is destiny's choice." Dylan grinned.

"And don't worry, I'm keeping my word. I won't use any Dragon-type legends today."

He spoke generously. When Dylan promised not to use Ultra Necrozma and the others, he meant it.

"No Dragon legends… but you've probably got other legends, right?"

Leon glanced at the screen — the turn order had already appeared. Dylan would move first.

"As expected of you, Leon. Sharp guy." Dylan gave him a thumbs-up.

Leon's mouth twitched.

"But relax, Leon. Your luck is great today. I'm running a pure Dark-type team."

Dylan unbuttoned two buttons at his collar and spoke calmly.

"And the first few won't be overpowered."

Yes. He'd brought a full Dark-type squad today. Pure. Authentic. Completely Dark.

Dark types?

Leon frowned. The dreaded ghosts, dragons, and fiends — Dylan's earliest teams.

Even without legends, Dylan's Dark-type teams were notoriously hard to handle.

"I just caught a few Dark types recently — perfect to try them out on you."

Dylan pulled out a deep red Luxury Ball.

The moment Leon saw it, his face stiffened. Dylan only used those balls for legendary Pokémon.

"With an opponent like you, it's good for them. After being sealed away for so long, they finally get to stretch their legs again."

The ball enlarged in Dylan's hand and he tossed it behind him.

"Wo-chien! To the field."

A heavy thud shook the arena.

A Pokémon resembling a sinister wooden deer emerged, lifting its head slowly, a massive cauldron resting atop it.

Its eerie aura seeped into the battlefield.

One of the Treasures of Ruin — Wo-chien.

"It's one of the Treasures of Ruin!"

Iono's eyes widened. Those were legendary Pokémon from Paldea's myths.

She had never seen one — they were sealed ages ago — but she certainly knew of them.

"Starting with Wo-chien, huh? Looks like even without those broken Dragon legends, Dylan's still going strong," Wallace whistled. Compared to "sympathizing" with Leon, he was more excited to see what else Dylan would bring out.

If Dylan used Kyogre against him, then surely he'd use even stronger legends against Leon, right?

Leon was way stronger than he was!

"A Dark + Ground Wo-chien?"

Leon thought for a moment before throwing out his first Pokémon.

"Rillaboom!"

His Rillaboom landed with a thud, pulling out its stump drum.

Grass beats Ground, and it had plenty of Fighting moves too.

"Good choice," Dylan nodded.

"Wo-chien."

A soft rumble echoed as a pale green aura seeped from Wo-chien's cauldron, washing over the battlefield.

Rillaboom shivered under it as a faint white glow flickered over its body.

Wo-chien's ability — Tablets of Ruin — reducing the opponent's Special Attack.

A very powerful ability.

"You alright, Rillaboom?" Leon asked.

Rillaboom shook its head — no issues. It felt weaker, sure…

But only in Special Attack.

And it didn't use Special Attack.

"Wo-chien, Sand Tomb."

Wo-chien stomped the ground, turning the terrain beneath Rillaboom into swirling sand that began dragging it downward.

"Fake Out!"

Rillaboom launched itself forward and slapped Wo-chien across the face.

Flinched!

"Wood Hammer!"

With a roar, Rillaboom glowed green and slammed forward.

Leon was fast — but Wo-chien reacted instantly.

"Pounce."

Wo-chien lunged, gleaming white.

Boom!

Wood Hammer and Pounce collided, blasting a shockwave across the field and cracking the arena floor.

Both Pokémon slid back.

Wo-chien dug its four hooves in to stabilize; Rillaboom braced itself with its palms, sliding before stopping.

"Fast reaction. Very high defense," Leon murmured, confirming his assessment.

Most importantly — its strength was within acceptable limits. Strong, but not broken.

Was this really something Dylan caught not long ago?

If so… that was wonderful.

Leon's eyes slowly brightened. It wasn't that he refused to be used as Dylan's background character — he'd already accepted that fate.

But as long as he got a good, passionate battle out of it, he didn't mind eventually facing those overpowering legends again.

After facing both Origin Dialga and Ultra Necrozma head-on…

What else was there to fear?

Sorry, but I, Leon, am a man who has seen the world.

No matter what Pokémon appears — I won't be shocked. (serious face)

(End of chapter)

---

# 512: Chapter 514: A Pokémon Born From Jealousy

The battle on the field continued with intensity. The clash between Wo-Chien and Rillaboom was fierce.

"Not bad at all for Champion Leon. He's holding his ground even against my brother's Wo-Chien."

Lillie watched Rillaboom exchanging heavy blows with Wo-Chien and couldn't help commenting.

"That's how it should be. Leon is one of the strongest Champions."

Blue muttered. Besides, Dylan hadn't put much focused training into the four Treasures of Ruin.

"And also…"

Blue tilted her head slightly, giving a sympathetic glance at Leon on the field.

She hoped he would stay strong.

On the field

"Return, Wo-Chien."

Dylan recalled the heavily injured Treasure of Ruin. Wo-Chien truly wasn't suited to fighting Rillaboom.

"That Rillaboom is impressive. But if the opponent is this one—"

He threw out his second Pokémon.

"Chi-Yu! Prepare for battle!"

"Chi-yu～"

A soft, ethereal sound echoed. A small Pokémon floated into view, its tiny body drifting gently in midair.

"Chi-Yu?" Leon's eyes narrowed slightly.

Its form resembled an ordinary goldfish—its body pale yellow, its tail patterned like dancing flames, and around each eye hung a pair of magatama.

**'Beep– Chi-Yu. Dark/Fire type. Ruin Pokémon.Ability: Beads of RuinMoves: Overheat, Infernal Abyss, Ruination, Smokescreen, Dark Pulse…

Description: The envy residing within cursed magatama sparked flames, and together they became this Pokémon.'**

"A Fire-type Treasure of Ruin?"

Leon recalled its info. He had never seen one in person, but its data existed in the Pokédex archives.

"That's right. Compared to Wo-Chien, Chi-Yu specializes in offense."

Dylan smiled.

"I see."

Leon immediately recalled his Rillaboom.

"If it's Fire-type, then you're up!"

He threw his second Pokémon.

"Inteleon!"

Inteleon landed stylishly—Galar's Water starter.

"Chi-Yu."

The magatama around Chi-Yu's eyes glowed blue, sending a faint light sweeping across the field.

Beads of Ruin—lowering the Special Defense of all other Pokémon.

Another extremely powerful ability.

Chi-Yu was the strongest special attacker among the Treasures of Ruin, and weakening an opponent's Sp. Def was a perfect match for it.

It was essentially a free damage boost every time.

"Inteleon! Snipe Shot!"

Leon wasted no time.

Inteleon bent its body and fired forward with incredible speed, fingers glowing with condensed water.

"Flamethrower!"

Chi-Yu darted upward, its magatama flashing red.

A blazing, massive flame in the shape of the kanji "大" surged downward.

Water and fire collided violently—Snipe Shot against Flamethrower.

But to the shock of everyone, the furious flames overpowered the water!

With a roar, the flames crashed onto Inteleon and blasted it backward.

Leon frowned.

"Whoa!! It's that strong?!"

Mei was stunned.

"That's Leon's Inteleon! And it got overpowered by a type disadvantage!"

"That Chi-Yu's special attack is terrifying," N said.

"Indeed. Inteleon's Sp. Atk is very high itself. Even with Snipe Shot being weaker than Flamethrower, overpowering it outright shows insane Sp. Atk."

Alder nodded.

"Compared to Wo-Chien, this Chi-Yu is much more aggressive."

"But Flamethrower shouldn't have done that much. Inteleon resists Fire, and it blocked the attack with Snipe Shot first."

Hilda analyzed.

As the steam cleared, a battered Inteleon emerged.

"Good."

Dylan nodded with satisfaction.

Even with Water resisting Fire, the Sp. Def drop from Beads of Ruin left Inteleon fragile.

Inteleon was a high-speed, high-Sp.Atk "assassin" type—naturally frail.

"Smokescreen."

Chi-Yu released a burst of flames and black smoke, filling the field.

"Surf!" Leon shouted.

Water surged upward, lifting Inteleon as it rode atop the sweeping wave.

Steam filled the arena as fire and water smashed together.

"Chi-Yu!"

Dylan called.

Chi-Yu drifted out of the smoke and into the air, scanning below.

"Snipe Shot!"

From the heated waters below, Inteleon burst upward—water glowing on its fingers.

This was a perfect chance. If Snipe Shot hit, Chi-Yu would take massive damage.

Leon was confident. Inteleon was one of his key partners.

"Ruination!!"

But Dylan had anticipated this.

He wanted Inteleon to strike.

From a dark sigil beneath Chi-Yu, black-red energy surged upward.

A devastating Ruination, the Treasures of Ruin's signature technique—pure destructive force.

Boom!

Snipe Shot hit true, sending Chi-Yu reeling.

But at the same moment, Ruination rained down on Inteleon like cursed droplets crashing from above.

The dark bombardment struck mercilessly, slamming Inteleon out of the air.

"Inteleon!"

Leon's expression tensed.

Inteleon crashed into the ground, curling painfully.

Dark didn't counter Water, but Inteleon's lowered Sp. Def was simply too low—and Chi-Yu's special attack far too high.

"Dark Pulse."

Chi-Yu followed up mercilessly.

"Protect!"

Leon barked.

Inteleon forced its trembling body to rise—green light beginning to form—

But could it finish in time?

Everyone held their breath.

BOOM!

Dark Pulse slammed down, exploding violently.

Leon's eyes narrowed. He hadn't seen a complete Protect barrier form…

As the dust faded, the crater revealed Inteleon unconscious in its depths.

Chi-Yu floated calmly overhead, its emberlike tail swaying.

"Inteleon is unable to battle. Chi-Yu wins!"

The referee announced.

Clearly, Protect had not activated in time.

"Chi-Yu is brutal," Steven murmured.

"Compared to Wo-Chien, its power is on a whole other level," Wallace agreed.

But that was natural—Chi-Yu specialized in high-speed, high-power offense.

Leon recalled his fainted Inteleon, eyes burning with determination.

Inteleon had done its job—it left the groundwork to defeat Chi-Yu.

"Dragapult!"

Leon sent out his third Pokémon—Galar's pseudo-legend.

Dark eyes narrowed, Dragapult glared at Chi-Yu.

"A Dragapult? Leon, you know Dark is strong against Ghost. Dragapult is at a type disadvantage."

Dylan raised a brow.

Was Leon banking on speed? A reasonable choice.

"But Dragapult can beat your Chi-Yu!"

Leon grinned.

"Dragon Darts!"

Dragapult lowered its head. The two Dreepy charged with dragon energy and shot forward.

"Very confident, I see. In that case—"

Dylan smirked.

"Flamethrower!"

The blazing flame struck the incoming Dragon Darts—

BOOM!!

—but the two Dreepy burst through the flames anyway, continuing onward—yet not attacking.

They flew back toward Dragapult.

Of course Dragapult itself wasn't beside Chi-Yu.

"Chi-Yu!"

Dylan instantly understood.

A dark tear opened behind Chi-Yu as Dragapult burst out—

Phantom Force!

Chi-Yu whipped its tail to retreat, but Dragapult was too fast.

BAM!!

The blow sent Chi-Yu tumbling, though Phantom Force dealt reduced damage.

The Dreepy returned to Dragapult.

"Dragon Darts!!"

Leon pressed the attack.

Dragapult shot forward with incredible speed.

"Fire Abyss!"

Chi-Yu's cry sharpened as flames roared upward, coloring the entire sky red.

Inferno.

Dragon Darts pierced into the sea of fire—exploding it apart—

"Dragon Rush!"

Dragapult barreled through the flames, a raging dragon aura taking shape as it smashed into Chi-Yu.

Chi-Yu shrieked as Dragapult drove it downward, crashing it into the field.

The ground shook violently.

"Amazing, Dragapult!!"

Hop jumped up.

Leon's heart pounded. This was the thrill he had long missed—

Could he do it?

Defeat one of Dylan's LEGENDARIES?

A feat only a rare few had ever accomplished.

(End of Chapter)

---

---

---

# 513: Chapter 515: Sob, Our Leon Finally Made Us Proud

"Not bad at all."

Dylan smiled and nodded. It was a beautifully executed string of fast attacks, and he had to admit—Leon's Dragapult was indeed strong.

"But if you think that alone is enough to defeat Chi-Yu… that won't do."

His words had barely fallen when an angry, sharp cry split the air.

A black-and-red shockwave erupted skyward.

Ruination!

Bursts of Ruination energy surged violently, blasting Dragapult backward.

Chi-Yu's body shimmered faint red as it floated upward, tail swaying.

"Dragapult!"

Leon's expression tightened. A counterattack already?

And Ruination was a Dark-type move—very punishing to Dragapult.

Dragapult forcibly steadied itself, its cold triangular pupils locking onto the Chi-Yu rising into the air.

"Dark Pulse!"

Black energy gathered before Chi-Yu, rippling outward in rings as wave after wave of Dark Pulse shot toward Dragapult.

"Phantom Force!"

Dragapult's body glided eerily sideways and vanished into a shadowy rift behind it.

There it was again.

Dylan narrowed his eyes slightly. Phantom Force—approaching while hiding the user—was terribly effective against a Special Attacker like Chi-Yu.

It could essentially get into melee range unharmed.

The next instant, a deep shadow fissure opened behind Chi-Yu.

"Chi-Yu—Smokescreen!"

Chi-Yu shrieked sharply as flames burst forth, thick black smoke erupting with them.

Fire-type Smokescreen!

Dragapult, just emerging from Phantom Force, was engulfed instantly.

But its body still glowed with ghostly light, pushing against the flames as it rammed itself hard into Chi-Yu.

"Dragon Darts Dive!"

Leon shouted.

The fire from Smokescreen burned fiercely, but Dragapult endured it, driving Chi-Yu downward at high speed.

Draconic energy condensed rapidly around them.

Roar!!!

A huge, savage dragon howled, slamming Chi-Yu into the battlefield with crushing force.

The ground rumbled, deep cracks splitting outward from the impact.

Dust filled the air.

Everyone stared, breath held—what happened?

A teal figure slowly rose, Dragapult panting slightly as it looked down.

The dust began to clear, and within a crater at the bottom—

Chi-Yu lay fainted.

"Chi-Yu is unable to battle. Dragapult wins!"

The referee declared.

"My goodness!! After a fierce exchange, Leon's Dragapult has defeated Dylan's Chi-Yu!"

The announcer screamed at the top of his lungs.

"Woooo—!!"

The stadium erupted—especially Leon's fans, who were practically delirious.

"Sob–sob! LEON! Our Leon finally made us proud!"

"He beat Dylan's Chi-Yu using only two Pokémon! THIS is Leon's true strength!"

"Uh… it sounds weird when you put it like that, but it's not wrong…"

"Those who got 6-0'd by Dylan—come out and SPEAK! Who's getting 6-0'd now?! WHO?!"

"Hahaha you're killing me—but yeah, Leon really does have skill."

Leon's fans were ecstatic.

The first time he fought Dylan, he was humiliated—completely swept, almost from start to finish.

That battle massively damaged both Leon's and Galar's reputation worldwide.

He was even placed low in the initial draft ranking of the Masters Tournament.

But battle after battle, Leon had slowly rebuilt his standing.

And today—matched against Dylan again—no matter what happened afterward, he had already beaten one of Dylan's legendaries!

A real legendary!

"This is impressive. Get some rest."

Dylan recalled Chi-Yu and spoke gently.

Losing didn't bother him; Chi-Yu's level wasn't very high, and losing to Leon was perfectly normal.

In fact, Dylan had already expected this when he decided to use the Treasures of Ruin.

"To defeat my Chi-Yu—Leon, that was wonderful."

Dylan smiled warmly at him.

"So then—how will you handle this one?"

Without waiting for Leon's reply, Dylan tossed out his third Poké Ball.

"Chien-Pao! Prepare for battle!"

"Pao—!"

A deep growl echoed as a gorgeous snow leopard burst from the light, landing before Dylan.

It tilted its head upward, icy gaze locking onto Dragapult.

Another of the Treasures of Ruin—Chien-Pao.

'Beep— Chien-Pao. Dark/Ice type. Ruin Pokémon.

Ability: Sword of Ruin

Moves: Sheer Cold, Sacred Sword, Ice Shard, Ruination, Sucker Punch…

Description: The grudges of those slain by the sword gathered upon the snow and became this Pokémon.'

Frosty breath seeped from Chien-Pao's snowy body. Its cold, merciless eyes chilled the soul.

Sword of Ruin activated.

Every Pokémon on the field (except itself) had its Defense lowered.

"Chien-Pao…"

Leon tensed. This one felt extremely dangerous—

More dangerous than Wo-Chien or Chi-Yu.

Its power—and difficulty to handle—was clearly higher.

"So Dylan really did capture all four Treasures of Ruin," Tulip remarked with a smile.

Better this way—the ancient texts said the Treasures invited calamity, and that was why they were sealed.

Now, under Dylan's control, they wouldn't wreak havoc.

That was good for everyone.

"Dark/Ice… so it's quad-weak to Fighting."

Nemona studied the data.

Not a great typing—lots of weaknesses, including a quadruple one.

But since Dylan sent it out as his third Pokémon, its strength was undoubtedly exceptional.

On the field—

"Dragapult, Dragon Darts!"

Leon decided to test its power first.

Dragapult shot out its two Dreepy like missiles.

"Sucker Punch."

A flash of dark light flickered in Chien-Pao's eyes.

Its white body became a streaking blur as it dashed toward Dragapult.

Its four-limbed sprint was lightning-fast—like a white bolt tearing across the arena.

So fast! Leon's eyes widened.

Dragon Darts could never hit something moving like this. Dragapult had to dodge.

"Dragapult!"

Dragapult retreated at high speed—but Sucker Punch had given Chien-Pao enormous momentum.

Chien-Pao reached Dragapult's position in an instant, leaping high, claws glowing black.

Rip!

The claws tore across Dragapult's body, sending it tumbling with a cry.

"Icicle Crash!"

Chien-Pao roared and sharp icicles formed overhead, dropping toward Dragapult.

"Dragapult!!" Leon shouted.

Dragapult couldn't dodge—too fast, too soon.

Boom! Boom! Boom!

Huge icicles slammed into it one after another, knocking it helplessly to the ground.

Chien-Pao landed smoothly, its cold gaze fixed forward as shattered ice glittered around it.

Dragapult lay unconscious—

Already weakened by Chi-Yu's Ruination, then hit by a Dark-type Sucker Punch and an Ice-type Icicle Crash.

All super-effective.

And its already-lowered Defense from Sword of Ruin meant it stood no chance.

"Dragapult is unable to battle. Chien-Pao wins!"

The referee announced.

"So strong!"

Iris couldn't help clicking her tongue at the clean, fluid KO.

"It caught him off guard. Leon definitely didn't expect Chien-Pao to be that fast."

N shook his head.

"It's clearly a high-speed, high-attack physical sweeper. Losing Dragapult hurt, but gaining that intel is valuable."

Otherwise, discovering this with another Pokémon could cost much more.

If unlucky, a full-HP Pokémon could be one-comboed.

"Cinderace!"

Leon didn't hesitate and sent out his fourth Pokémon.

The athletic, handsome Cinderace landed before him.

"Using Libero to change types?" Dylan chuckled.

Naturally. Chien-Pao was 4× weak to Fighting—any good trainer would capitalize on that.

"But simply turning into a Fighting type isn't enough to beat my Chien-Pao, Leon."

Dylan spoke casually, like stating a fact.

Even with a 4× weakness, you had to land the hit.

If Chien-Pao couldn't be touched, type advantage meant nothing.

"That's not necessarily true, Dylan."

"Oh?"

Dylan raised a brow.

Leon grinned and lifted Cinderace's Poké Ball, recalling it.

His Dynamax Band flared red, scarlet energy swirling into the Poké Ball—

Gigantamaxing!

Once—twice—three times!

The ball expanded massively. Leon hurled it upward with all his strength.

A black vortex churned open in the sky.

Cinderace emerged, inflating three times before standing tall atop a massive flaming ball—Gigantamax Cinderace.

"No matter how fast you are—Max Moves almost never miss!"

Leon declared. This was his countermeasure.

Indeed, relying on Fighting moves with Libero alone wouldn't guarantee victory.

Chien-Pao was too fast, too strong, too explosive—a nightmare for unprepared Pokémon.

But—

Gigantamax solved that.

Wide-area Max Moves made dodging almost impossible.

"I see." Dylan looked up at the towering Cinderace.

"Come then, Dylan. Will you keep fighting? Or switch Pokémon?"

Leon asked eagerly.

He wanted to see what else Dylan had prepared.

(End of chapter)

# 514: Chapter 516: To Show My Respect

"Against a Gigantamax Cinderace, that Chien-Pao really can't fight."

Blue rubbed her pale chin as she looked at the massive, imposing Gigantamax Cinderace standing in the arena.

Once a Pokémon Gigantamaxes, dodging Max Moves becomes nearly impossible due to their sheer range.

And sending a Dynamaxed Chien-Pao to trade blows with a Gigantamax Cinderace would be outright foolish.

Chien-Pao was 4× weak to Fighting. Even Dynamaxed, it wouldn't matter—forcing a trade with Gigantamax Cinderace would only end badly.

After all, Chien-Pao was a high-speed attacker. Legendary or not, it was still fragile.

"Come back, Chien-Pao."

Dylan obviously wasn't that dumb. He recalled it immediately.

"As expected of you, Leon. In that case, I'll go with this."

Dylan sent out Wo-Chien again.

Letting Chien-Pao fight was impossible, and the last of the Treasures of Ruin—Wo-Chien—wasn't appropriate either.

Its Dark/Grass typing meant a 4× weakness to Bug, and Cinderace could learn U-turn.

There was no way Wo-Chien would survive a Bug-type Max Move from Gigantamax Cinderace.

So Wo-Chien was the best choice to face it head-on.

"Wo-Chien again…"

Leon's eyes flickered. Wo-Chien's Ground-type moves were super effective against Cinderace.

But no matter—Libero meant Gigantamax Cinderace could change its type.

"Wo-Chien!"

Dylan recalled Wo-Chien and activated his Dynamax Band, funneling energy into its Poké Ball.

Wo-Chien was released again—one pulse, two pulses, three pulses—

It grew larger and larger until Dynamaxed Wo-Chien towered over the field.

Two massive Dynamax Pokémon now faced each other. The visual impact alone made the crowd erupt with excitement.

Dynamax battles always set the blood boiling—two colossal monsters clashing with enormous, visually explosive moves… impossible not to hype people up.

But to be honest, Dynamax battles were also the most straightforward.

Because of the huge attack ranges, dodging was nearly impossible. Most Dynamax battles devolved into trading Max Moves with almost no technique.

And this time was no exception.

Dynamax Wo-Chien unleashed Max Quake repeatedly at Gigantamax Cinderace.

Leon, meanwhile, had Gigantamax Cinderace counter with Max Knuckle, pounding Wo-Chien in return.

And the final outcome—

"Wo-Chien and Cinderace are both unable to battle!"

The referee announced.

"What an unexpected result! Max Knuckle from Cinderace and Max Quake from Wo-Chien landed at the same moment—both have been taken out!"

The announcer's booming voice echoed through the stadium.

"Yes!! This means Leon has defeated two of Champion Dylan's main Pokémon!"

"And they were legendaries too! Our Leon is way too strong!"

"Don't do this, Champion Dylan! Please just end it with your dragons—if this continues I'm going to start believing Leon can win!"

"Leon is lucky to have fans like you…"

"But seriously, does Leon have a shot at beating Dylan's third legendary? Oh man, I hope he can!"

The crowd buzzed with excitement.

"That's the second one!"

Leon watched Dylan recall Wo-Chien, his heart pounding with exhilaration.

He had already defeated two of Dylan's main Pokémon! And both were legendaries. Truly worthy of being himself!

But he wasn't in a rush—Leon believed he could defeat one more! If he managed that, he would have beaten three of Dylan's legendary mains. He would be the true second-strongest under Dylan!

"Then I'll be counting on you again, Chien-Pao."

Dylan sent Chien-Pao back out.

The cold snow leopard landed silently before him.

Leon hesitated. Of his remaining Pokémon, none were especially suited to fight Chien-Pao.

Charizard? Absolutely not—Charizard was his ace. He would only use it when he had no other choice.

And truthfully, Mega Charizard X wasn't especially great against Chien-Pao either.

Even though Mega Charizard X was powerful, its Speed wasn't enough.

"Aegislash, battle!"

After some thought, Leon sent out Aegislash.

The eerie, floating sword Pokémon materialized before him, its eye beneath the hilt glaring at Chien-Pao.

"He's really using Aegislash against Chien-Pao? Another self-imposed type disadvantage."

Blue raised a brow. Leon was truly bold.

"Probably no other option left. And Steel resisting Ice isn't bad," Giovanni said calmly.

Though Ghost-type wasn't effective on Chien-Pao, Aegislash could use Sacred Sword, which was super effective.

"And honestly, it's not as hard as it looks. If Aegislash can withstand the opening assault and counterattack, Chien-Pao won't take it well."

Giovanni continued analyzing. Chien-Pao was a fast attacker—its defenses were low.

If Aegislash managed a defensive counter into Sacred Sword, it could potentially one-shot Chien-Pao.

Of course—that was only in theory.

Landing Sacred Sword on Chien-Pao in practice would be incredibly difficult.

And… Aegislash also had that move.

On the field—

"Aegislash, huh? Interesting." Dylan narrowed his eyes.

"Chien-Pao!"

Under Sword of Ruin, Aegislash's Defense dropped.

Seeing Leon not attacking first, Dylan struck.

"Sucker Punch!"

Chien-Pao dashed forward, lightning fast.

"Aegislash, Air Slash!"

Leon ordered back.

Aegislash unsheathed itself and swung, unleashing a blade of pressurized air.

Chien-Pao slipped past it effortlessly, then leapt high, claws glowing poison-purple.

Sucker Punch!

"King's Shield!"

Aegislash instantly raised its shield, forming an absolute defense.

But—

Chien-Pao's Sucker Punch swept past from the side, avoiding the shield entirely.

A feint?!

The entire audience froze.

Of course—if Chien-Pao attacked head-on, King's Shield would not only block the attack but also lower Chien-Pao's Attack. Dylan would never allow that.

Leon's expression tightened. He had foreseen this possibility.

"Sacred Sword!"

Aegislash unsheathed again, its blade glowing blue as it swung toward Chien-Pao.

A perfect opportunity—Chien-Pao had no footing midair!

"Aerial Ace!"

Chien-Pao twisted its long body backward as if doing a backflip.

With Flying-type Aerial Ace, it forcibly corrected its position midair.

Sacred Sword sliced just past its tail tip—missing.

Leon's pupils constricted.

Bam!

Aerial Ace slammed into Aegislash. Even with Steel resisting Flying, Aegislash was in Blade Form—its defense was paper-thin.

It staggered.

Chien-Pao seized the moment.

"King's Shield!"

"Taunt!"

Leon shouted desperately—but Dylan was faster.

No!

Leon felt his stomach drop.

Chien-Pao growled with a mocking, sinister edge—Taunt!

Aegislash froze mid-motion, furious.

King's Shield was sealed. And all status moves as well.

Dylan even predicted the timing…

"Hell's Spike!"

With Taunt successful, Chien-Pao raised a glowing black claw.

The Dark-type Hell's Spike struck Aegislash hard.

Aegislash crashed to the ground, unable to float.

Chien-Pao stayed on top of it.

"Sacred Sword!"

Leon tried again desperately.

"Sucker Punch!"

Chien-Pao's claws struck before Aegislash could move.

Aegislash fell limp.

"Aegislash is unable to battle. Chien-Pao wins!"

The referee declared.

Leon stared, speechless.

"You also had Destiny Bond, right?" Dylan asked with a smile.

"So you really figured it out."

Leon gave a helpless laugh and recalled Aegislash.

"Of course. I train Aegislash too, after all."

Dylan shrugged. He'd used Taunt not only to block King's Shield, but also to prevent Destiny Bond.

He knew the tactic well.

"You're too strong, Dylan!"

Leon's eyes were blazing with excitement. Dylan was exactly the kind of opponent he loved.

"Then next—my ace will take the stage!"

With burning passion, Leon threw his sixth Poké Ball.

Rillaboom was already weakened and no match for Chien-Pao—so it was time for Charizard.

ROAR!!

Charizard spread its wings and let out a powerful cry before landing in front of Leon, staring fiercely at Chien-Pao.

Leon's ace—Charizard!

"This is only the second time I've seen it, but your Charizard really has presence," Dylan said with a grin.

"Hahaha—Charizard is my pride!"

Leon was clearly thrilled. Even fighting legendaries, this battle was still incredibly fun!

"And since that's your ace, out of respect—I'll bring out my ace too."

"…???"

Leon froze.

Those words triggered a painful flashback—from the Hammerlocke Stadium—of that dragon of radiant light…

But Dylan had promised he wouldn't use Ultra Necrozma today.

Yet—

Leon's eyes widened as Dylan recalled Chien-Pao and reached toward the special Poké Ball hanging over his chest.

"Wait! STOP! Dylan—what are you doing!?"

(End of chapter)

# 515: Chapter 517: I Once Fought Arceus!

Leon felt a powerful and very ominous premonition.

Dylan always wore a Poké Ball over his chest like a necklace.

But very few people actually knew what was inside it.

Leon was one of the few aware of its existence, because back then Wallace had asked about it out of curiosity.

Dylan answered them honestly—but at the time, the Poké Ball had been empty. Dylan said he simply hadn't found a Pokémon worthy of being captured with that ball.

But now…

"I've always been a trainer who respects his opponents."

Dylan took down the Master Ball from his chest—the Master Ball expanded in his hand.

"If my opponent uses their ace, then I must respond with my own."

Dylan smiled warmly.

Leon: (︵)

Leon's mouth twitched uncontrollably.

"A Master Ball…"

Giovanni watched with interest. He knew exactly what a Master Ball was.

He had been present when the president of Silph Co. personally handed it to Dylan, proudly calling it "the pinnacle of Poké Ball technology." Giovanni had always been curious what Dylan would eventually capture with it.

But now he was even more curious what Dylan had actually stored inside.

Everyone knew Dylan kept his legendary Pokémon in Luxury Balls—even legends like Necrozma and Kyurem.

So what Pokémon could possibly be worthy of a Master Ball?

"Leaf, Lillie—do you two know what's inside that ball?"

Giovanni couldn't contain his curiosity and asked.

Kieran, Ilene, and the others also looked over.

Lillie's delicate face froze with an odd mixture of hesitation and conflict.

"Umm… you'll find out in a moment."

Leaf finally answered. After all, Dylan was about to release it.

On the field—

"Dylan, I think Chien-Pao is fine."

Leon said as politely as he could. He wanted to keep fighting Chien-Pao!

Who knew what nightmare was inside that Master Ball?!

"Believe me, Leon—today will be the proudest day of your life. Your name will be remembered for centuries!"

Dylan smiled brightly.

Leon: "???"

"After all… you'll be the only trainer to ever face this on a battlefield."

"Because it's you, Leon, I want to use it."

Dylan said no more—he simply flicked his wrist and threw the ball.

"Arceus! It's your turn!"

The Master Ball opened midair, and a white Pokémon glowing faintly with gold light descended slowly. Its divine gaze made Charizard's entire body erupt with goosebumps.

A soft golden radiance clung to Arceus's body—an overwhelming, sacred aura that no one dared look at directly.

It was so strong it shook the soul.

Charizard swallowed hard and unconsciously stepped back.

What WAS this creature?!

Its body wasn't even large, yet it completely crushed Charizard's fighting spirit. Just the thought of attacking it felt like sacrilege.

Even though Charizard had faced Ultra Necrozma and felt true despair, this was something else entirely.

This wasn't "too strong to beat."

This was "too divine to even swing at."

"Arceus…?"

Leon stared at the majestic being. He could sense its extraordinary nature, but he didn't recognize it.

— Beep! Arceus. Normal-type. The Original PokémonAbility: MultitypeMoves: Judgment, Hyper Beam, Perish Song, Future Sight…Mythology says it existed before the universe itself.

Leaf, Kieran, and the others froze after reading the Pokédex entry. Cynthia's eyes widened in disbelief.

Arceus… Arceus!!

"Dylan actually… captured Arceus?!"

Cynthia trembled. In Sinnoh mythology, Arceus was the Creator.

The creator god! Was this a joke?!

More and more people scanned the entry; gasps of shock spread through the stadium as everyone stared at Arceus with awe and reverence.

"My goodness! Unbelievable! Champion Dylan is using Arceus—the mythical Pokémon said to have CREATED THE WORLD!"

The announcer nearly screamed.

After all, Arceus's Pokédex entry was too outrageous.

So outrageous people wondered if it could be real.

"You see? Now everyone understands who you are, Arceus," Dylan said cheerfully.

"…"

Arceus only sighed softly and gazed at the trembling Charizard with warm eyes.

"Dylan, you…"

Leon finally heard the announcer's voice clearly and stared at Arceus in shock.

The creator… god…?

"It's true. Arceus really is the creator. And by the way, Dialga, Palkia, and Giratina were all created by Arceus."

Dylan confirmed.

Leon's jaw dropped.

If Dylan said it, then—

It was real.

But the problem was…

You want me to FIGHT ARCEUS???

Leon glanced at the divine being before him, then at Charizard.

He fell silent.

He once believed Ultra Necrozma was the strongest opponent he'd ever face. Never did he imagine the creator of the world would stand across from him.

And in a stadium!!

"Of course, I said I'd use a pure Dark-type team today. Arceus is normally Normal-type, but that's easy to fix."

Dylan looked at Arceus.

Arceus's eyes glimmered. A flash of black light rippled across its body as it activated the Dread Plate.

In an instant, all its golden sections turned pitch black. Even its thousand arms glimmered with darkness.

Dark Arceus.

A suffocating wave of malice poured from its body. Charizard nearly collapsed under the pressure.

But it lasted only a moment—Arceus quickly withdrew its malice, its crimson eyes soft and warm again.

"Perfect. A Dark-type Arceus. Now my Dark team is complete."

Dylan nodded with satisfaction.

Leon fell into deep thought.

"Come, Leon! I'm ready. Let our aces battle in a fair, spectacular showdown!"

Dylan extended his hand sincerely.

"I…"

Leon almost choked. He looked at Dylan like he was insane.

Dylan—are you even HUMAN?!

"This chance only comes once in your entire life, Leon. Look at the Pokémon standing before you."

Dylan urged him patiently.

Leon froze.

"The opportunity to face the Creator itself doesn't come twice. And this is an official match. If you turn this down… won't you regret it forever?"

Dylan knew Leon had to get fired up. Surrendering right away wouldn't feel right.

"Once in a lifetime…"

Leon was wavering. Yes—it was utterly absurd.

But thinking differently…

What was he being defeated by?

Arceus.

He would be defeated by ARCEUS.

This was something to brag about for a lifetime.

"Charizard!!"

Leon's eyes brightened. Yes—there was nothing to fear. This was history.

Charizard: "…"

Leon was fired up, but Charizard still felt its legs shaking.

Especially knowing it had to breathe fire at Arceus. Charizard's throat felt like sandpaper.

"Excellent! I knew you were the right man, Leon!"

Dylan beamed.

"Come! Show Arceus the bond you share with Charizard!"

"That's right! Charizard! Show Arceus our TRUE POWER!"

Leon's passion burned. He lived for battles like this.

"Charizard! Blast Burn!"

He was so excited he forgot to Mega Evolve—but it hardly mattered.

Charizard trembled, staring at the black-and-white embodiment of divinity.

Was it really supposed to attack… THAT?

It tried to breathe fire—but nothing came out.

This wasn't life-or-death. This was a sparring match. Charizard's perspective was very different.

Arceus gave Charizard an encouraging look.

Come. Show me your strength.

Charizard suddenly felt a powerful surge rising inside.

"ROAR!!"

It soared upward, breathing a massive inferno down—

Blast Burn!

The blazing scarlet flame lit up the entire stadium.

A powerful Blast Burn indeed—expected of Leon's ace.

However—

A faint golden barrier appeared before Arceus.

Blast Burn exploded against it harmlessly.

"Judgment."

Dylan's command was almost gleeful.

Arceus lifted its head, forming a dark sphere of energy and launching it skyward.

BOOM—!

The energy burst overhead, releasing dozens of meteors trailing black fire.

Dark-type Judgment.

"Protect!" Leon shouted.

Charizard raised its barrier, but it didn't matter. Even though Arceus had suppressed its power, Protect still couldn't endure.

Meteor after meteor crashed down.

The shield broke.

Charizard was smashed into the ground by the blasts, unable to move.

Another Judgment struck.

When the smoke cleared—

Charizard was unconscious.

A complete one-hit destruction.

"Whew—!"

Leon exhaled deeply. Honestly, he wasn't surprised. Ultra Necrozma had done the same to him.

But this time—it didn't matter.

Because he had immunity from elimination, and how many people could claim THIS?

From now on, he could proudly tell anyone:

I once fought Origin Dialga… Ultra Necrozma… and Dark Arceus. Who are YOU to compare with me?

And as the only man to ever face Arceus in an official match—

Maybe his name really WOULD be remembered forever.

Thinking about it that way—

This was absolutely worth it.

(End of chapter)

# 516: Chapter 518: Do You Want Leon to Die?

"One hit… it was over…"

"Kidding me? That was Arceus. It's even scarier than Ultra Necrozma last time."

"LEON!! You're a real man!!"

"I keep saying Leon is the bravest Champion—any objections? Every time he fights Dylan it turns into a top-tier match!"

"He's unbelievable… no words."

The stadium was bursting with excitement, and the internet had already exploded.

Arceus—the Creator itself—had appeared. And Leon had attacked it head-on.

"Dylan is getting more outrageous by the day."

In the stands, Wallace couldn't help clicking his tongue. It was one thing to bring out powerful legends before—but Arceus?

He actually pitied Leon.

"Good lord, that's brutal." Leaf's companion Blue whistled in disbelief.

"No wonder he kept it in a Master Ball—too strong."

Giovanni nodded in admiration. A Pokémon like Arceus truly deserved nothing less than a Master Ball.

Red: "…"

"Is our Gym Leader really this busted? He can even befriend that kind of Pokémon?" Selene's little face scrunched up. She was proud—very proud—but also deeply conflicted.

Damn it! If Gym Leader Dylan is that strong, wasn't her dream of surpassing him going to be delayed for a lifetime?

"Arceus isn't exactly Dylan's Pokémon," Leaf said casually, watching the footage she had just recorded. Right—she had filmed everything.

This was a once-in-thousands-of-years moment. Arceus had personally battled on a human field!

"Champion Leon is extraordinary. To face Arceus directly—and even attack—that's incredible," Lillie said admiringly.

To stand before Arceus and still summon the will to fight… that was true courage.

"There are always mortals brave enough to face the radiance of a god," Leaf murmured, already thinking of a video title. Perfect.

"In a way, Leon truly is lucky," Giovanni joked lightly. "People will talk about this for decades—maybe centuries."

The first World Championship was already massively popular.

Now add Dylan battling Leon, and the appearance of Arceus.

This match was guaranteed to be remembered.

Whenever people thought of Arceus, the noble Creator—

They would think of Dylan and Leon. Dylan for using Arceus…And Leon—

Well, he was the unfortunate backdrop. But a backdrop worthy of respect—because he had faced Arceus head-on, without surrender, and even breathed fire at the Creator.

"Charizard is unable to battle. Arceus wins."

The referee glanced nervously at Arceus—now reverted to its normal Plate—and announced the result.

"Since all six of Leon's Pokémon are unable to battle, the winner is Dylan! Dylan advances!"

The arena erupted.

"Champion Dylan is unbeatable! No—invincible in heaven and on earth!"

"Poor Leon—haha! But still, this time he did better. At least he took down two of Dylan's powerhouses."

"I've heard the myths about the Creator… but seeing Arceus with my own eyes? Unreal."

"Hahaha! This ticket was worth EVERYTHING! I'm printing my photo of Arceus and framing it!"

"Um… does anyone else feel like Arceus looks strangely familiar?"

"Arceus, please bless me! Turn my Sylveon into a human girl! I'll do ANYTHING!!"

Arceus—who had sharp ears despite pretending otherwise—heard several "Please bless me, Arceus" pleas.

It had originally intended to bestow blessings on a few genuinely devout humans.

But—

"Turn my Sylveon into a human girl"…left Arceus tilting its head in utter confusion.

What was the point of that? What did this human even want?

"What an exhilarating battle!" Dylan sighed happily.

Leon, who had just walked over, felt his eyelids twitch hard.

But Dylan honestly meant it. Mostly because battling with Arceus was exhilarating.

"Leon, how was it?" Dylan asked.

"A good battle."

Leon glanced at Arceus hovering behind Dylan. Up close, he could feel its overwhelming divinity even more clearly.

It was breathtaking.

Arceus wasn't even large—but its presence was overwhelming.

And truthfully, before Arceus appeared, Leon had enjoyed the match. No despair, no bottomless gap—just intense, exciting combat. It was the kind of match he had expected from fighting Dylan.

And beating a few of Dylan's Pokémon after giving everything he had—that part felt great.

"When you have time, we can fight again. I still have several Pokémon I didn't use."

Dylan offered his hand with a smile.

"No need. You can go find someone else," Leon said seriously, shaking his hand.

There was no doubt Dylan's remaining Pokémon were all abnormal.

Leon had suffered enough. No one else suffered like he did.

"Haha, just kidding. I hope you win your revival match. Maybe we can meet again later."

Dylan wished him well.

Since Leon was in the Master Tier, he had one immunity from elimination. But he still needed to win a revival match to reach the Top 8.

And a revival match was never easy.

Still, with Leon's strength, it wasn't impossible—same for Lance, who had lost to Giovanni.

"Don't worry. If we battle again, I won't use Arceus. It only agreed to help me this one time."

Dylan promised.

Arceus's "trial card" was over—and Dylan genuinely regretted losing it.

Arceus shot him a sideways glance, amused.

Leon: "…"

Leon was silent.

You think I believe you?

Last time Dylan told him he wouldn't use broken Dragon-type legends.And then he sent out the Creator Alpaca.

Maybe he technically didn't lie…But who knew what else Dylan had hidden???

On the battlefield, Leon absolutely did NOT want to meet Dylan again.

The match ended, and discussions soared.

Next up was the final match of the Top 16: Gloria vs Nemona!

These two had history.

At the end of the ranking phase, Gloria had challenged Nemona—who held the last Master Tier seat—and defeated her, taking her place.

Now they faced each other again.

Truly fated rivals.

One was Galar's "prodigy." The other was Paldea's rising star.

The hype was enormous.

Dylan watched with interest—Galar's protagonist vs Paldea's protagonist. Another matchup of "broken builds."

"I wonder if Gloria can win Nemona again this time."

Leon looked worried. He dearly hoped Gloria could reach Top 8.

Gloria had won last time, but barely. Their strength was extremely close—one mistake and everything could collapse.

Soon, Gloria and Nemona entered the field, both girls' eyes burning with fighting spirit.

No words needed—just battle.

They already knew each other's teams well. Victory would depend entirely on adaptation and new tactics.

The match was fierce and brilliant.

But in the end, as before, it came down to Hop's Urshifu vs Nemona's Meowscarada.

And once again, Meowscarada fell.

"Meowscarada is unable to battle! Urshifu wins!"

"The winner is Hop! Gloria advances!"

Nemona stared at her fallen Meowscarada, bit her lip, and lowered her head.

She had lost again.

"…Sigh."

In the stands, Rika exhaled helplessly.

Nemona was very strong—but so was her opponent.

"I win! Hmph! I inherited Leon's will! I refuse to fall here!"

Gloria cheered proudly, punching the air.

The camera immediately cut to Leon.

Leon could only smile awkwardly.

Inheriting his will??He just lost a match—he didn't die.He still had a revival match!!

"Ahaha—Gloria is certainly amusing," the announcer laughed, smoothing things over.

"And now, we have our eight official Top 8 qualifiers!"

Red, Giovanni, Selene, May, Dawn, Yellow, Dylan, Leon.

Eight portraits filled the big screen.

"But it's not set in stone yet! Two more trainers can still challenge these spots!"

"That's right—Lance and Leon!"

Their portraits also lit up.

"As Master-Tier trainers, they each have one immunity from elimination. They will be randomly paired against one of the eight qualifiers. If they win, they will replace their opponent and enter the Top 8!"

The announcement caused quite a stir, though no complaints—after all, they earned this privilege.

The real issue was…

"Challenging the current Top 8? Honestly… that's not easy."

"Lance and Leon are still extremely strong. They can win their revival matches."

"It depends entirely on the random matchups. And the randomizer is truly random."

"Exactly. And among the Top 8… is Champion Dylan."

"Which means they could be matched against Dylan."

"If Leon's revival match pairs him with Dylan…"

"…Are you trying to kill him?"

(End of chapter)
