# 517: Chapter 519: Dragon Legion of a Senior!

Leon and Lance's revival matches were scheduled for the next day.

As for the two Champions' matches, Dylan's group didn't particularly worry.After all, it was still random matching—there was no guarantee either Champion would be matched with them.

Dylan aside, Red and Selene stayed calm.If they did get matched against Lance or Leon, so be it—they would simply beat them.And if they lost, it only meant their strength wasn't enough.

Giovanni, on the other hand…

Yes.He was hoping Lance would be matched with him again.He wanted to personally send Lance home for good.

Yellow, however, was actually nervous. She knew very well—both Lance and Leon were incredibly strong.If she got matched with either one, it would be a difficult battle.But Yellow had already resolved herself: no matter what, she would advance—until she met her Gym Leader brother on the battlefield.

Before that, she refused to lose to anyone.

Meanwhile, the internet was ablaze with discussion over Lance and Leon's revival matches.Some people even wrote huge detailed posts analyzing which matchups would allow the two Champions to succeed.

Videos and discussions flooded every platform.

But ultimately, it all depended on tomorrow's random match.

The next day, the venue was still the Indigo Arena.Even though only two revival matches would be held, the hype remained enormous.Lance and Leon were both deeply beloved Champions—their fanbases alone made today unmissable.

If they won, they could still enter the Top 8.

"Welcome, everyone, to the Indigo Arena! Next, we will begin Lance's revival match!"

The announcer's voice was a bit hoarse already—understandable, considering how many days he'd spent shouting at full volume.

After today, he finally got a few days of rest.

"This is a privilege Lance earned during the ranking phase by stabilizing his position in the Master Tier. If he wins against one of the current Top 8, he will replace them."

"Will Lance succeed? Let us watch closely!"

He raised his arm. Lance's cold, sharp portrait lit up on one side of the screen.

On the other side, the portraits of all eight qualifiers began to spin.

Who would it land on?

Lance stood with arms crossed, expression unreadably calm.

"Wow, so lucky—they get a revival match," Nemona said, a little jealous.

"That's something Champion Lance earned. It's fair," Rika replied.

If Master-Tier trainers were allowed to skip straight into the Top 8, that would've been unfair to everyone else. This rule was already a compromise.

"I know, but I can still be jealous! If only I had made Master Tier before the fixed brackets were set…"

Nemona pouted.She almost had the strength for it—but Master Tier had become too competitive near the end.

If she had made it, she'd have this chance too.

"…"

Blue remained silent, looking a bit crushed inside.

"I wonder who Lance will be matched against this time," Wallace mused, touching his pale chin.

"Hopefully not Selene. Champion Lance is a formidable opponent," Steven frowned.

"It's fine! I'll win for sure!" Selene declared, proud and confident.

"No. We're about to enter the Top 8—fighting an extra match right now only exposes more of your weaknesses," Steven said seriously.

Selene blinked.

"Your battle habits, your tactics, your Pokémon's style—all of these can be studied and countered. Being matched with Lance right now is only harmful, not helpful."

"O-oh…"

She finally understood.

On the screen, the spinning portraits gradually slowed.

And finally—

A girl in a white dress and straw hat appeared.

Yellow.

"…Ah."

"That's not ideal."

Leaf and Lillie froze.

"So it's me…" Yellow murmured, clenching her hands as she stared at Lance's cold expression.

Dylan raised an eyebrow.

"Yellow, huh. Running into Senior Lance—must be destiny."

He shook his head. There was no Suicune Island incident in this world… yet destiny still brought them together here.

"It's fine, Yellow. You can do it. Champion Lance isn't scary. Believe in yourself," Selene comforted her junior.

Yellow was nervous, but nodded.

"That's right. Trust yourself—and your partners. They'll respond to your call," Dylan said warmly.

"If he stands in your way, then go all out and surpass him. As long as you have courage, you'll find you can do much more than you think."

"…Yes! I understand!"

Her racing heartbeat slowly calmed. She nodded firmly.

Lance could be defeated.

She still remembered what Dylan told her when she first joined the Rota Gym—One day, defeat Champion Lance.

Now she finally faced him.

On the field—

"Let us welcome our two trainers!!"

The announcer shouted with renewed energy.

"LANCE!"

"YELLOW!"

Two figures stepped out of opposite tunnels.

The blood-haired man in a cape—like a dragon himself—and the pure, straw-hat girl in a white dress.

Their presences were worlds apart.

"This is just unlucky…"

"Feels like Lance could make her cry with one punch…"

"She beat Hilda and Rosa before, but against Champion Lance? Can she really do it?"

"Looks like Lance will win the revival match."

"Hey! Don't underestimate Yellow! She's Champion Dylan's disciple!"

"Exactly—and you said 'disciple,' not Dylan himself. If it were Dylan, then sure—he'd beat Lance easily."

The livestream chat scrolled nonstop.

Yellow's previous two battles had amazed many people.But against Lance, most believed she had no chance.

Lance was famous, powerful, and a Master-Tier trainer.

Yellow had beaten Hilda and Rosa—but barely, and with explosive gambits from her partners.

Could she really win again?

"That girl…"

Rosa grit her teeth. She had boasted before the match… but after losing, she had silently remembered Yellow forever.

"Can she defeat Lance?" Touko murmured. She knew Yellow was unusual—after witnessing their match firsthand.But this time, her opponent was Lance. Could she really overturn fate?

On the field, the referee finished the pre-battle checks and stepped back.The screen's pointer spun rapidly.

Although Yellow had found her resolve, standing before Lance still made her tense.

The man was like a dragon—aloof, proud, his presence overwhelming.His sharp eyes alone made her breath hitch.

Can I really defeat Champion Lance…?

The thought resurfaced again.

Defeat the man once called "Earth's strongest dragon master"?

"You're very nervous."

The sudden cold voice froze her.

"S-sorry…"

She responded instinctively.

"Why apologize?" Lance asked flatly.

"Ah? I…"

"Raise your head."

Yellow bit her lip, eyes watering slightly, but obediently lifted her head.Lance stood before her, arms crossed, gaze calm and piercing.

"What are you afraid of?" he asked.

She trembled, wanting to look down again.

"I said—raise your head. Look at me."

"…"

His tone carried a faint edge—just enough to stiffen her spine.

"You are Dylan's disciple, chosen by Viridian Forest just like us. Does facing me—a senior—terrify you so much?"

Yellow shook her head weakly.

"No… it's because…"

"Because you're afraid of losing to me?"

She had no rebuttal.That was what frightened her.

Facing such a powerful opponent made her anxious and uneasy.

"To fear before the battle begins—did Dylan teach you that?"

Lance's sharp gaze cut straight into her.

"No!" Yellow blurted.

"Then why tremble? You can't even look your opponent in the eye."

Lance's tone remained calm.

He liked Yellow's potential.In fact, he was pleased another exceptional 'chosen one' had appeared—like Dylan.

But now, on the battlefield, he could see her hesitation and doubt.

"I…" Yellow looked ashamed.

"No matter how strong the opponent, as trainers we must charge forward. Even if the gap is enormous—you must not lose the courage to challenge."

Lance's voice was cool and steady.

"Even if there's only a one-in-ten-thousand chance—will you not even try? If you're this weak, how do you face the Pokémon who fought for you until now?"

"If this is your resolve, you cannot become strong. Dylan misjudged you, and Viridian Forest must have been blind."

Yellow sucked in breath after breath—her eyes gradually sharpening, trembling fading.

"I understand! Senior Lance—please guide me!"

"Hmph. Now that's more like it."

The pointer stopped.

Lance got the first move.

"Yellow—let me show you. As your senior, I'll let you witness the Dragon Legion that once ruled the world!"

He enlarged a Poké Ball in his hand, lips curling in a cold smile.

Show me your full strength—your unique Viridian power.

(End of chapter)

# 518: Chapter 520: Is that ability of yours even legal?

Lance had more or less guessed it—Yellow was special. Her Pokémon were strong, and even among Champion-tier trainers she wasn't weak.

But what made him pay closer attention was the fact that Yellow, like him, carried the blessing of Viridian Forest. In Lance's eyes, she was the same kind of special as he was.

A junior of his, essentially. And since Yellow was also Dylan's disciple, Lance took her even more seriously.

So he had watched all of her battles carefully.

And from that, he'd noticed something subtle—something all of Yellow's battles had in common.

Whether it was Hilda or Rosa, both had stronger raw power than Yellow. From the very start of combat, Yellow's Pokémon were almost always at a disadvantage.

But as the fight went on… Yellow's Pokémon would suddenly become much stronger.

That wasn't something ordinary battle momentum could explain—and Lance had felt the pulse of Viridian power.

If Yellow hadn't actively invoked Viridian power, how could that surge appear?

Yet by his own understanding, Viridian power only allowed healing, sensing emotions, and communicating with Pokémon.

But Yellow was clearly different!

"Salamence!"

Lance decisively threw out his first Pokémon.

"Rooaaar!!"

A brutal draconic roar split the air. A fierce, domineering Salamence spread its blood-red wings, glaring down at Yellow.

The pseudo-legend of Hoenn—Salamence!

"Come, Yellow! Let me feel your power for myself!"

Lance's icy voice carried heavy intimidation.

But Yellow now more or less understood—this senior had a cold exterior and a warm heart. And he seemed to value her quite a bit.

Even though rumors painted him as arrogant and difficult, after a short exchange, Yellow felt he was actually a very kind senior.

He just didn't talk nicely—but that didn't bother Yellow.

After all, Silver had a similar personality. Yellow was long used to cool, sharp-tongued people.

"Yes! I definitely won't let you down!"

Yellow answered firmly.

"I still want to battle with Dylan, so, Senior Lance—I'm going to defeat you!!"

Her voice rang with will and conviction.

Lance's lips curved slightly—clearly pleased.

"Hmph—defeat me? You're underestimating my Dragon Legion."

Even if he acknowledged her resolve, he still scoffed.

He admitted Yellow had talent—and some kind of special ability—but defeating him?

That was wishful thinking. Did she really think he was like Hilda or Rosa—someone who could be toppled by a temporary power spike?

Impossible.

"Slowking, I'm counting on you!"

Yellow inhaled deeply and threw her Poké Ball.

Light flashed, and a composed Slowking appeared, hands folded behind its back, looking calm and unhurried.

A standing Slowpoke with a white shell-crown on its head.

"Water type? Planning on using Ice moves?"

Lance didn't react. He'd long grown used to these matchups.

Water and Ice practically went together—most Water types could use Ice moves. Everyone knew that when fighting Lance, Ice coverage was mandatory.

Especially after his battle with Dylan had been broadcast. The whole world now knew how to counter him.

But Lance only wanted to laugh.

Yes, his team was heavily weak to Ice—

But only if the Ice-type attacks were strong enough. Otherwise, "super-effective" was meaningless.

Just like now.

"Slowking, Ice Beam!"

Yellow launched the first attack.

Ice gathered in Slowking's mouth, firing a twisting beam upward at Salamence.

"Fire Blast!"

Lance's cloak swept as he commanded sharply.

Salamence released a blazing, character-shaped fire pillar.

The collision erupted mid-air—fire smashing against ice. For a moment they clashed violently…

Then Fire Blast began overpowering Ice Beam, pushing it back until it exploded against Slowking.

Boom!

Flames surged. Slowking groaned in pain.

Luckily it resisted Fire-type moves, so it could endure the hit.

"So strong…!" Yellow gasped. As expected of one of Lance's main partners—its special attack was terrifying.

"Using Slowking is correct," Giovanni commented from the stands, "but the power gap is too wide."

Ice coverage or not, Lance had too much experience fighting Ice tactics.

And if the power gap was big enough, type advantage became laughable.

After all, Lance wasn't stupid nor a third-rate trainer who'd allow you a clean hit.

And many times, he simply smashed through Ice attacks with raw force.

Just like now.

"Ice Beam" got blasted aside.

But the attack wasn't over.

"Dragon Rush!"

Lance ordered.

Salamence folded its wings and dived—dragon-energy swirling wildly around it.

Boom!

"Slowking!" Yellow cried.

The flames had barely faded when Dragon Rush struck. Slowking tried to dodge—too late.

Another explosion. The arena trembled. Salamence again rose from the dust.

On the ground, Slowking struggled—barely able to move.

"Is that all?"

Lance folded his arms, frowning slightly at Yellow's worried face.

He didn't press the attack.

"With this level of strength, you plan to stand before Dylan?"

"Or were your declarations empty words with no resolve behind them?"

Yellow flinched, eyes widening.

"I promised Dylan—I'll reach him! I will!"

"And with what?" Lance asked coolly. "Your weakness? Or your fragile Pokémon?"

"You're not as good as Selene. Maybe not even that eliminated Silver."

Lance knew Yellow was stronger than Silver.

But right now, he wanted to push her.

She claimed she would defeat him. She claimed she would move forward.

But that unique power of hers hadn't responded at all.

Was her resolve still lacking?

"...…"

Yellow trembled.

"You can't beat me. Give up," Lance said coldly.

"No! As long as my partners and I stand together, our bond can overcome anything!"

And create miracles!

Yellow's eyes hardened.

"Slowking!"

A mysterious pulse flooded Slowking's body—Viridian power—strengthening it dramatically. Slowking straightened, blue psychic light flickering in its eyes.

I feel… terrifyingly strong!

Slowking gazed up at Salamence—its psychic force thick and oppressive.

"…Oh?"

Lance immediately noticed the shift—and from up close, he felt the Viridian surge far more clearly.

So he had been right.

"Interesting. Very interesting. You really are more special than me."

A slow smile spread across Lance's face.

A junior like this—far too fascinating.

"Senior Lance! I'll show you—I'm not weak! And my Pokémon aren't weak either!"

Yellow met Lance's increasingly excited stare.

"Hahahaha! Come, then!"

Lance laughed. His provocation had worked—and now it was fun.

He wanted to see just how strong Yellow's Pokémon could become.

As for losing?

Impossible. He didn't believe her boosted Pokémon could surpass his dragons. That was impossible.

He'd watched her battles—yes, they grew stronger, but within reason.

A temporary power spike beating his Dragon Legion?

Ridiculous.

And then…

"Dragonite is unable to battle! Pikachu wins!"

"Lance has lost all six Pokémon—victory goes to Yellow! Lance is eliminated!"

The referee announced the result.

Lance stared at his fallen ace Dragonite, utterly silent.

"I won!"

Yellow panted lightly, her face pale—using too much Viridian power always drained her.

Normally she could push lightly, but this battle had been far too intense. And…

Against Lance, light boosting was nowhere near enough.

"…I actually lost…"

Lance's brow twitched. Replaying the sheer absurdity of Yellow's power spikes, only one thought filled his mind:

Is that kid's ability even LEGAL???

(End of chapter)

# 519: Chapter 521: Kanto Is Too Absurd

"Hohoho~ This is too interesting. Lance actually lost to Yellow."

Giovanni's face carried an uncontrollable smile—this was hilarious!The invincible Lance, defeated by a newcomer who only began traveling this year—ha!

"Yellow's kind of insane… she can boost her Pokémon that much?" Blue was dumbfounded.

He could guess Yellow had some special ability that strengthens her Pokémon…But beating Lance? That was ridiculous.

"This is what they call talent."Dylan chuckled. Yellow's ability was definitely among the strongest—something unique to her alone.

On the other side—

Watching Yellow and Lance finish their match, Hilda and Rosa exchanged confused looks.

"She's definitely cheating!"Iris finally reacted, pointing at Yellow with wide eyes.

Now it was basically confirmed—Yellow had an ability that temporarily boosts Pokémon strength.

"Truly gifted," Alder praised.

"Indeed… this ability is outrageous," N agreed.

In truth, the concept of temporarily strengthening Pokémon wasn't exclusive to Yellow.Powerful psychics could boost Psychic-type Pokémon to some degree—for example, Sabrina, who was said to be able to force Psychic-types to temporarily evolve.But even that was limited—and far weaker than this.

Yellow's Slowking should have been completely overwhelmed by Lance's Salamence.Yet after her power activated, Slowking could fight evenly—and even defeat it with Ice Beam.

This wasn't something you could just call a "burst of power."These were Champion-level Pokémon—Champions vary hugely in strength.But Yellow made her entire team "burst" at once.

"No wonder Dylan accepted her as a student," N sighed.

On the field—

"Thank you, Pikachu~!"

"Pika~!"Yellow hugged her partner, who leapt into her arms affectionately.

Even with her power boosting their strength, Lance's Ace Dragonite was overwhelmingly strong.Pikachu had used everything it had to win.

Step—step—Yellow looked up and saw Lance approaching, Dragonite already awakened behind him.

"Senior…"

"You did very well."

Lance looked at her for a moment, then—rarely—showed a faint smile.

"Your partners are very strong."

"Thank you!"

Yellow sensed his kindness and blushed shyly.

"Your ability… is truly special. Keep going."Lance paused, then added,"I think you'll make it to Dylan in the end."

"Yes! With your support, I'll work even harder!"

Yellow was full of fighting spirit. She felt that since she'd even defeated Lance, there was nothing left to fear!

"Heh~"Seeing her bright smile, Lance couldn't help a soft laugh.

But the next moment, he returned to his cold demeanor.

"You've defeated me to advance. If you lose to some nobody later… I won't forgive you."

"Eh?"Yellow blinked.Was there even a "nobody" left among the final eight?

Thus, Yellow's battle with Lance concluded—with the shocking result of Lance's elimination.

Fans were devastated, but… what could they do?He first drew Giovanni, head of Kanto's Elite Four,then Yellow, Dylan's disciple—all from Kanto.

Talk about bad luck!

With Lance out, next was Leon's resurrection match.

Half an hour later, the screen began drawing his opponent.

"Please, Arceus, I beg you!""Give Leon a good matchup!""Anyone but Dylan!""No—anyone but Kanto! Please!"

Chat was full of Leon fans praying or complaining.

After Lance's experience, everyone realized resurrection matches were dangerous—Your opponent might look harmless, but secretly be another "cheat-user."

Especially those Kanto people—Dylan, Giovanni, Red, Selene, and now Yellow, who had just clapped Lance.

There were five of them.Even the Top 4 didn't have enough room for all five!

Finally, the spinning portraits slowed…

And stopped on a blue-haired girl with a white barrettes.

"It's me…"Dawn stared at the screen, her pretty face collapsing.

"Unlucky," Cynthia muttered.

"It's fine, Cynthia! I won't lose!"

Dawn bounced to her feet, fired up.

"Be careful—Leon is extremely strong," the Champion warned.Since the start of the tournament, aside from Dylan and Red, Leon had never lost.

"No worries~ Worst case, I lose once. No big deal!" Dawn laughed.She wanted victory—but she wasn't obsessed.She didn't have Yellow's ridiculous ability anyway.

"That's good. Don't stress.And besides… Sinnoh is already famous enough."Cynthia gently fixed Dawn's bangs, like a big sister.

Arceus appearing had made Sinnoh world-famous.After all, Sinnoh was the land said to be shaped by Arceus itself.

Meanwhile—

"Dawn from Sinnoh, huh?"Leon studied the cheerful girl's portrait.

Hop exhaled in relief."At least it's not someone from Kanto. Big bro, beat her and you're in the Top 8!"

Leon nodded, but didn't underestimate her.Anyone who reached the Top 8 was elite.

Half an hour later—Leon and Dawn stood on the battlefield.

The indicator pointed to Leon for first pick.

"Dawn, it's fine if I call you that, right?" Leon asked kindly.

"Ah? Sure, Leon!" Dawn smiled.

"As your senior… fighting you for a Top 8 spot feels a little embarrassing," Leon admitted.Honestly, he'd rather fight Giovanni.

"Haha, it's fine! If I can't beat you, then I don't deserve the spot anyway," Dawn said brightly.

"What a refreshing heart you have."Leon genuinely admired her.

"Then let's battle at full strength, Dawn!"

His eyes burned with fighting spirit.Aside from Dylan, Leon believed he could rival anyone.Even Red—if they fought again, he might not lose.

And surely…Dawn couldn't have some ridiculous temporary-power-boosting ability, right?

(End of Chapter)

# 520: Chapter 522: The Eight Masters!

Leon and Dawn's match began.

Unsurprisingly, the battle was extremely fierce—Dawn truly lived up to her reputation as Sinnoh's "monster."

Her strength fully justified the title, and even Leon found himself locked in a dead-even struggle with her.

"She's really strong… I wonder if she might actually beat Leon."

Leaf clicked her tongue. Other regions clearly had geniuses too.

Even though she herself was gifted, Leaf admitted she wasn't Leon's match right now.

If she fought him, it definitely wouldn't look like this. Dawn was stronger than her… but whatever—she had someone backing her up.

Fighting wasn't her preferred thing anyway. She liked battles she could win with her mouth.

"Hard to say. She's Dawn, not Hikari," Dylan said, shaking his head.

Although the battle looked even, Dylan could tell Leon had gradually taken control.

As a top-tier Champion, Leon's strength was simply overwhelming—he had reached the very peak of Champion level.

Dawn was also top-tier now, but in a direct clash with Leon, she was still at a slight disadvantage.

They might all be "monsters," but this world's main characters weren't as broken as in the games.

If it were Hikari, though, the situation would be entirely different.

Yes—if that walking cheat Hikari had two years to train, defeating Leon would be like swatting a fly.

"Who's Hikari?" Leaf blinked.

"Ummm… hard to explain. Just think of her as a super prodigy."

Leaf: (—)

Leaf really wanted to complain: could anyone be more monstrous than you, Dylan?

As the battle neared its end, even though Dawn fought with everything she had, she still lost—her Mega Garchomp was defeated by Charizard X, and the match slipped from her hands.

"Garchomp is unable to battle. Charizard wins."

"All six of Dawn's Pokémon can no longer continue. Leon is victorious and advances!"

The referee announced the result.

The arena erupted in cheers—along with plenty of disappointed groans.

Dawn had many supporters. Sunny, cheerful girls were always popular.

"Aw~ what a pity…"

Dawn recalled her fallen Garchomp and sighed deeply.

She'd mentally prepared for this, but losing for real still stung.

Even Red would vanish with a "teleport" when he lost—probably embarrassed.

"A wonderful battle! Dawn, you're a very strong opponent."

A warm yet excited voice reached her.

"Congratulations on advancing, Leon."

Dawn looked up to see Leon offering his hand, so she smiled back.

Even as the loser, she could be gracious.

"Thanks. Dawn, feel free to visit Galar anytime—let's train together."

Leon liked strong juniors like her. Her bright personality only made her more likable.

And most importantly—she was strong enough to fight seriously.

"Okay! I'll definitely visit!" Dawn's blue eyes sparkled.

"Good luck in the next matches, Leon! You beat me, so you better keep going!"

"I will."

"Hehe~ let's hope you don't meet Champion Dylan again in the Top 8!"

Leon froze… then forced a polite smile.

Does she know how to talk or not!?

"The final match has concluded! Leon defeats Dawn and successfully emerges from the resurrection bracket—entering the Top Eight!"

The host's booming voice filled the stadium.

"Our eight trainers have all assembled! Let's take a look!"

One by one, full-body portraits appeared on the giant screen, each standing before their ace Pokémon.

Leon — Charizard

Yellow — Pikachu

Gloria— Urshifu

May — Swampert

Giovanni — LandorusSelene — Incineroar

Red — Charizard

And finally—

Dylan — Arceus

"WOOO—!!"

The whole crowd roared wildly.

When taking the photo, Dylan had struggled—he wanted Ultra Necrozma behind him, since it was his first "beyond-overpowered" legendary.

But Arceus was more famous now, and the Creator God definitely had more style.

Arceus even cooperated, lifting its head majestically behind him for the photo.

Perfect.

"These are the eight trainers of the World Championships' Top Eight!"

"They've defeated challenger after challenger to reach this point—they are the world's strongest 'Eight Masters'!"

"But the real battles begin now!"

The host's voice grew more heated as he raised his arms.

"Next comes the Masters Eight Tournament! We will witness the birth of the strongest!"

Thunderous screams shook the arena as the main tournament concluded, making way for the ultimate event.

"Tch—he's really going all out with the hype."

Blue snorted at the flushed, screaming fans.

"The host is very good at stirring emotion. No wonder Chairman Goodshow picked him," Giovanni said with a calm smile.

Even his steady heart felt a bit fired up.

Red said nothing. His crimson eyes under his cap brim burned fiercely.

"Now it's your turn to shine! Leaf and I will cheer you on," Leaf chirped happily.

Though she'd been eliminated, it didn't matter—her man and her former coworker both made the Masters Eight!

…well, except one person.

Blue stared mournfully at Red… then at everyone else.

Damn it!How come he was the only Kanto Elite Four member who didn't make it!?

Was he really the weakest!?

Blue was very sad. Blue wanted to sit alone in a corner.

"Hehe~ I wouldn't mind fighting Dylan again. Our last match was last year, wasn't it?"

Giovanni's tone was calm but amused.

Champion?

There was no point thinking about that. Who could possibly beat Dylan?

But fighting his way into the finals and battling Dylan again?Giovanni would enjoy that.

And honestly—with Dylan competing—second place was basically "champion" anyway.

"Hey hey hey! Giovanni-uncle, I refuse to let that slide!" Selene protested immediately.

"I'm the one who'll reach the finals! The one who'll challenge Big Brother Dylan is me!"

Selene raised her chin proudly, radiating an aura of absolute supremacy.

"And excuse me—but Dylan, stand up a moment."

She seemed to recall something and said it directly.

"You little brat…"Dylan sighed, but still stood up cooperatively.

Selene was very satisfied. With everyone else sitting, she could look down at them all—even if it was just barely.

She cleared her throat twice.

"In my humble opinion—every one of you is mid. I am the undefeated queen here!"

"The finals? Already reserved. Anyone object?"

If they wanted to show off—she could show off too!

The bold declaration left Dylan speechless.

Blue: "???"

Yellow: "…"

Red: "…"

Giovanni: "…"

Leaf and Lillie stared in shock.

Giovanni slowly turned to Dylan, stunned.What on earth have you been teaching this child?

Dylan's eye twitched violently.NOT THIS! Definitely not this!

"Ha? Selene, you—!"Blue shot to his feet.

"Sit down. You didn't make the Masters Eight. I wasn't talking about you."

Selene waved him off calmly.

Blue's face went green.

"Um…" Yellow felt she should cheer herself on too.

"Shimei, don't. If you want to fight me, go train two more years."

Selene waved at her as well.

Yellow: (︵)

"Red! Say something!!"

Blue jabbed Red desperately.He didn't have the right to talk back to Selene, but Red should!

Selene was practically stepping on their faces—how could Red take this!?

Trash-talk king Blue wished he could be Red's mouthpiece.

"…"

Red's lips moved slightly, but he didn't know what to say—so he stayed silent.

Blue died inside.

"You see? Only Uncle Giovanni here is worth anything.But now is the era of us younger trainers."Selene's hands went to her hips as she stood proudly.

Dylan didn't even know how to comment anymore.

"Heh~ Selene, I am the head of the Kanto Elite Four," Giovanni said lightly, implying much more.

(End of Chapter)

# 521: Chapter 523: This Kanto Champion…

The freshly revealed Masters Eight quickly became the hottest topic around the world.

And when people saw that five of the eight were trainers from Kanto, the entire world collectively fell silent.

Well… what could anyone even say?They got in with sheer strength.Nothing to complain about.

All anyone could hope now was:Please don't let the Top Four also be all Kanto.

Aside from Kanto's monstrous lineup, the most popular region at the moment was undoubtedly Galar.

Chairman Rose's smile was about to twist off his face.Because aside from Kanto, only Galar and Hoenn had made it into the Masters Eight.

And Galar even had two trainers in it!

Of course, everyone knew that the World Championships title was guaranteed to go to Dylan.That much was obvious.

But getting second place would practically be a championship of its own, right?

Beating Dylan was impossible, but beating the other contestants?That was still doable!

Rose felt very confident. After all, both Gloria and Leon were extremely strong!

A week passed in the blink of an eye.

More and more people gathered at the Indigo Plateau.Even though most regional Champions had already been eliminated, it didn't dampen the enthusiasm for the World Championships.

A competition showcasing the strongest of the strongest—Of course people loved watching it!

The Indigo Arena was once again packed to the brim.Today, the Masters Eight Tournament would finally begin.

The sacred flame burned brightly.The livestream opened, and bullet comments flooded the screen instantly.

"Front row! Damn it—I failed to grab tickets again!""Hurry up and start! I want battles! BATTLES!""Waited seven days for this! Time to watch Champion Dylan destroy people!""'Fighting for the championship,' more like 'fighting desperately for second place.' I wonder who'll get it.""That second place is obviously going to be Leon!""As if! With Leon's luck? I wouldn't be surprised if he ends up meeting Dylan again today."

The livestream was exploding with chatter.

The arena atmosphere was just as heated, because atop the stage stood the slick-backed host.

"Hellooooo everyone! Welcome to Indigo Arena!"

After a week of rest, his voice sounded even clearer and more energetic.

"Today we begin an even fiercer stage—the Masters Eight Tournament! Out of these eight trainers, only four will advance to the semifinals!"

"Who will break through? Let's find out together!"

He smiled broadly.

"Now then, let us begin the random matchup draw! The system will handle everything to ensure fairness."

…No.The only fair thing would be to ban Dylan and just place him directly into the finals.Let the rest of us fight for that one open slot.

Leon's face remained expressionless.This mighty Leon had nearly failed to enter the Top Eight earlier—if not for the resurrection round!

Of course, he was only venting.

He looked up as the giant screen began to spin through contestant portraits.

"Arceus, please! Don't let big bro and Gloria fight each other!"

Hop prayed desperately.Galar only had two candidates—an internal fight right away would be tragic.

Let the Kanto folks fight each other instead.They had five people!

"I wonder who the first group will be."

Wallace watched with anticipation.The Masters Eight had only eight trainers, meaning only four battles total.

Winners would enter the semifinals immediately.

"No idea, but a Kanto-versus-Kanto matchup is very likely," Steven said thoughtfully.

With five Kanto trainers, the probability was simply too high.

And even with absurdly good luck, at least one Kanto internal fight was guaranteed.

Finally, the first portrait stopped—a handsome, confident smile.

Leon!

"Me? Again?"

Leon blinked.His face had been appearing far too often lately—fight Dylan, fight the resurrection match, and now first up in the Masters Eight.

But that was fine.As long as the opponent wasn't Dylan, everyone else was about the same to him.

Under countless tense, excited, and curious stares, the second portrait began to slow—

A young girl in a flower straw hat, pure and beautiful, appeared.

One of Kanto's Elite Four—Selene!

"Woooh!!"

The arena exploded with cheers.

This match would definitely be amazing!

"Leon, huh? He's a tough opponent."

Selene gazed at Leon's portrait thoughtfully.

Although Leon always suffered miserably when fighting her gym big brother, even she had to admit that Leon was incredibly strong.

A true powerhouse!

"What's wrong? No confidence?"Dylan teased.

"Hehe~ I'll make him understand that not only can he not beat my teacher—he can't even beat me!"

Selene puffed out her impressive chest proudly.

A strong opponent, yes—but she had absolute confidence.Outside of her gym big brother, she was the strongest in Kanto.

Rumor said that after the World Championships, her gym big brother would "retire" from the Champion position.Which meant…

The Kanto Champion's seat… could be hers.

If she wanted that title, she had to shine here.

This Leon came at just the right time.

On the battlefield.

"The two contestants are ready! Let's welcome the competitors!"

"Leon!"

"Selene!"

The host's voice boomed across the entire arena.

From both contestant tunnels, two figures emerged—and the crowd erupted.

"Selene冲鸭!!""If you can't beat your teacher, you can at least beat him! Champion Leon, show that girl what cruelty means!""This first match alone is worth the ticket price!""Champion vs. Elite Four—easy win!""I'm betting on Selene. No reason, I'm just a degenerate."

Both had gained a huge following by now.No surprise—these were the strongest eight trainers in the world.

Selene and Leon stepped into their trainer platforms.

"The battle will now begin! The competitors are Selene from Kanto, and Leon from Galar!"

The referee entered to announce the rules.

"The rules are: a full 6-on-6 battle. Three power-ups are permitted. When all six Pokémon on one side can no longer battle, the other side wins."

"The winner advances. Loser is eliminated."

He looked left and right, then dropped his arm.

"Battle—start!"

The rules were identical to earlier rounds, except for one big difference—

All three power-ups were now allowed.

Mega Evolution.Dynamax.Terastallization.

As for Z-Moves…

Well, Z-Moves were hard to evaluate.They were strictly inferior to Dynamax in real battles.

And unlike in the games, Z-Moves weren't guaranteed to hit in this world.Hard to dodge, yes—but not unavoidable.

And worst of all—

Z-Moves had that embarrassing pre-move dance.

By the time you finished posing, your opponent had already prepared three countermeasures.Who would stupidly wait for you to fire it?

Mega Evolution = huge power boost.Dynamax = triple giant attacks + boosted durability.Terastallization = type advantage flexibility.

These three were simply too perfect.Z-Moves stood no chance.

The referee withdrew.

The big screen pointer spun—and landed on Selene.

"Ooh~ I'm going first? Not bad."

She tilted her head, then smiled brightly and held up a Poké Ball.

"Leon, sen— Leon, please go easy on me."

Her voice was sweet, her smile even sweeter—a perfect sweet little idol.

"Haha! Don't worry about that—give it your all, Selene! Whether you or I advance, it's impossible to say right now!"

Leon laughed boldly, battle spirit blazing.Selene was someone he took more seriously than Dawn.

Her strength had been demonstrated many times—as Dylan's top disciple.

During the ranking tournament, Selene had gone on a rampage, even defeating Dragon Master Lance to take his rank.

Leon viewed her as a true equal.

"Well then, I'm very sorry, Leon… because I intend to send you out of the tournament. This victory will be mine."

Selene's smile grew even sweeter—but her words didn't match the tone at all.

"Oh?"Leon raised a brow and grinned wider.

"Such confidence? Then prove it. If you can defeat me, advancing will be rightfully yours. But…"

He lifted his right hand toward her, an overwhelming aura surging from him.His cape billowed behind him.

"I am Leon! The Champion of Galar!"

New blood pushing out the old, huh?Too bad—

I, Leon, don't get pushed so easily.

I will win!!

(End of Chapter)

# 522: Chapter 524: Truly Wronged!!

"Leon versus Selene, huh."

Dylan watched the unfolding match with great interest.He had to admit—this was definitely a fight worth watching.

Leon's strength needed no explanation, and Selene's current power was just as frightening.

Especially because Selene wasn't only strong through her Pokémon—her tactics were endlessly flexible, downright cruel, and perfectly executed.

Fighting Selene was… a terrible experience.

"I wonder if Selene can take down Leon?" Giovanni narrowed his eyes at the field.

On the field

"No matter what monsters or demons stand in my way—I'll wipe them all out!"

Selene's sweet expression vanished, replaced by a sharp, feral grin.

She tossed out her Poké Ball.

"Gengar!"

"Jie-jie~!"

The grinning Gengar opened its mouth wide, its face full of sinister delight.

"Mega Gengar, huh?"

Leon immediately understood.Gengar was Selene's most iconic lead Pokémon—and infamous for good reason.

Even more importantly—Gengar was her Mega slot.

Shadow Tag paired with Mega Gengar's burst power made it one of the most nightmarish Pokémon to face.

"Yo~ Little Leon is in trouble now. That Gengar is a nightmare."Mustard stroked his chin proudly from the stands.

"Honey, who do you think has the better chance of winning, Selene or Leon?"His wife, Honey, asked curiously.

"I really couldn't say, dear. Their strength is so high… Even at my peak I couldn't beat either one of them~"Mustard sighed dramatically.

"That's not necessarily true. Your undefeated streak from your youth still hasn't been broken," Honey said gently.

"Hahaha! That doesn't count for much~ Besides, the era back then can't compare to now!"

Mustard waved dismissively, but he was clearly enjoying himself.

"It's already amazing I get to watch such battles at my age. Totally worth it~!"

Honey smiled while Mustard's excited gaze returned to the match.

On the battlefield

"Dragapult!"

Leon chose his ace without hesitation.

Mega Gengar's Shadow Tag prevented switching, its raw power was monstrous, and it possessed countless bizarre utility moves.

In every sense, Mega Gengar was the perfect "nightmare."

Leon thought for a moment, then decided Dragapult was his best bet—Ghost vs. Ghost, both super fast, and Dragapult could immediately get close with Phantom Force.

The battle began.

Just as Leon expected, Dragapult used Phantom Force to appear right beside Mega Gengar.Closing distance was normally extremely difficult—but Phantom Force solved the problem.

However—

Just getting close wasn't enough to beat Mega Gengar.Especially Selene's Mega Gengar.

During that instant of approach, Selene successfully landed a Will-O-Wisp.

Dragapult's Clear Body couldn't prevent the Attack drop from Burn.

A burned Dragapult had far less bite—and eventually, Mega Gengar defeated it.

But Mega Gengar had taken damage too.Not that it mattered—since HP was never its real problem.

Leon's second Pokémon, Cinderace, came out swinging—

Only to immediately eat a Pain Split.

Equalizing their HP.

Leon was now definitely at a disadvantage.

"I have to say, Selene's control of Gengar is terrifying."

Blue whistled softly.

"It is… Selene's Mega Gengar is really hard to fight."Yellow nodded seriously.

Speed, Special Attack, Shadow Tag, Curse, Toxic, Hypnosis, Pain Split, Perish Song…And when all else failed—Destiny Bond.

A Gengar that refused to die quietly.

"I don't think Cinderace can beat that thing either."Giovanni frowned after watching for a while.

He was already thinking—"If it were me, how would I deal with this Mega Gengar?"

Even among trainers of equal ability,a one-sided match could still happen.

Leon, unable to handle Mega Gengar, was falling further and further behind.

Though Cinderace changed to Dark-type with Libero,it still fell to Mega Gengar.

Two straight losses.Leon's expression grew solemn.

"Leon has to counterattack, or this will get ugly for him,"Steven muttered.

Gengar was worn down for sure—but with Pain Split, it could recover anytime.

He couldn't let it run wild.

Leon understood this well.

His third Pokémon—Aegislash—came out and Dynamaxed immediately.

Selene, however, didn't stubbornly overextend.She recalled Mega Gengar and sent out Venusaur.

Gigantamax Venusaur.

The two gigantic Pokémon faced off.

Gigantamax Venusaur's body glowed faintly red, the flower on its back blooming huge, two thick vines reaching forward.

It looked… honestly kind of goofy.

But looks didn't win battles.

Selene used Max Quake, Max Guard, Max Quake.Forcing out Aegislash's Dynamax turns.

And both Max Quakes did meaningful damage.

When the giants returned to normal size, Selene brought Mega Gengar back.

Aegislash and Mega Gengar ultimately went down together.

Yes—Aegislash used Destiny Bond.

Leon's desperate backup plan.He hated using it, but removing Mega Gengar was worth it.

The match heated up greatly.

Leon finally sent out his ace—Mega Charizard X.

Selene still had three main Pokémon left, including her fully healthy Incineroar.

And then—

Selene stopped acting human.

Mega Charizard X was indeed strong.

But what if Incineroar Intimidated it three times…and used Parting Shot three times?

Leon's face turned greener and greener with every stat drop.

"This is ILLEGAL—!!"

"How is it illegal? That's called strategy. And Selene was being merciful. She didn't Intimidate from start to finish."

"First he gets beaten by Dylan's Arceus… then by Selene's evil-style torture… Leon, you poor soul…"

"Leon definitely wants to punch someone. His invincible Charizard got turned into a fluffy plushie."

"Three Intimidates + three Parting Shots. Even Leon's Charizard can't take that."

The crowd buzzed wildly.

This level of tactical abuse—on the world's biggest stage—was incredible to watch.

"Charizard is unable to battle. Incineroar wins."

"Leon has lost all six Pokémon.The winner of this match—Selene!"

The referee's voice echoed.

"It's decided! After a tense and spectacular battle, Selene has defeated the mighty Leon and becomes the first trainer to enter the semifinals!"

The announcer's voice boom across the stadium.

"Kyaaa~! I did it!"

Selene bounced happily, fists clenched in delight.

She said she wanted to reach the finals and fight her gym big brother…but that was just trash talk.

Now that she had beaten Leon and entered the Top Four, she was just one step from her goal.Of course she was thrilled.

Her adorable excitement was displayed on the big screen, warming the hearts of many spectators.

Such overwhelming strength—yet still just a cheerful girl.

"Congratulations, Selene."

Leon walked up with a gentle smile.

"Thank you~"Selene beamed.

"Fighting you was really fun! I hope we can battle again someday."

She felt she should say something appropriate for the winner.

"…"

Leon froze.He wanted to speak, but the words wouldn't come.

He wanted to say:

"No need! There is absolutely no need!!"

He didn't mind tactics, but today he had truly felt what suffering meant.

No wonder she was Dylan's student…

Leon knew Dylan used to be called the "Cruel Gym Leader."But when he fought Dylan, it was always high-level combat—Dylan never had the chance to show that "cruelty."

But the pain Leon didn't get from Dylan—

He got ALL of it todayfrom Dylan's student.

Truly… a sin.

(End of Chapter)

# 523: Chapter 525: Trust Me

The battle between Selene and Leon had come to an end, and to everyone's surprise, Selene actually defeated Leon and advanced.

Though perhaps, to Selene's fans, this outcome was only natural.

After all, Selene had already beaten Lance in the ranking rounds, and then defeated N—who wielded Zekrom—in the top-16 match.

That alone had earned her a massive wave of supporters, and many even believed Selene would be the runner-up.

Now that she had become the first person to reach the semifinals, her fans were naturally thrilled.

"I wonder who my opponent will be."

Giovanni stared at the remaining six portraits on the giant screen. One of them was his own.

8-to-4 meant only four matchups, and now that Selene had advanced, only three pairings remained.

As long as he didn't get matched with Dylan, Giovanni figured advancing to the semifinals shouldn't be a problem.

But…

Giovanni glanced sideways at Red, his deep eyes flickering.

As a fellow Elite Four member, Giovanni knew Red quite well. To be honest, Red was no less dangerous than Selene.

Quiet most of the time, but once a battle began, he was terrifying. A powerful opponent.

Half an hour later, the pairing for the second match began.

As the portraits spun rapidly across the giant screen, everyone's eyes were locked on it.

The first portrait gradually stopped—a boy in a cap appeared.

Red!

"Red, huh? That guy's a tough one," Steven muttered, expression tightening.

"Absolutely. That kid's a monster on par with Selene," Wallace agreed.

Kyogre bless them. Hopefully May wouldn't get matched with him. Otherwise… who knew what would happen.

Wallace prayed silently to the Sea King—but then froze.

Even if he didn't want May to face Red, who could she confidently beat?

Giovanni? Leaf? Serenity?

None of them were easy matchups. Dylan didn't even need to be mentioned.

At this stage—top eight—there were no weaklings left. Any opponent would be strong.

Still, Wallace sincerely hoped for an internal Kanto matchup—Dylan.

The second portrait stopped. A cute girl with a bow-shaped hairband appeared.

"It's me~!"

May blinked, then broke into a bright smile.

Steven and Wallace exchanged helpless looks. Nothing they could do—there were only so many competitors. Anyone could get matched.

"Do your best, May. You're not weaker than him," Steven encouraged.

"Mm-hmm~ Of course! Don't worry, Steven, I won't lose!"

May was fired up—she was the last hope of Hoenn!

And since she was fighting someone her age, she absolutely didn't believe she would lose. She was undefeated among Hoenn's young trainers!

"Good. That mindset is important. But don't push too hard—you've already done wonderfully," Steven said gently.

"Got it~"

Elsewhere—

"The Hoenn girl, May, really is strong."

Leaf recalled her data. A 'monster-class' trainer of Hoenn's generation—very powerful indeed.

"So, Red? You think you can win?" Leaf asked with a teasing smile.

Red's crimson eyes were blazing with fighting spirit. The match hadn't even started, but this kind of opponent set his battle-longing heart on fire.

"Hmph—how could he not be confident? That girl beat me to get here," Blue snorted before Red could answer.

"Red, if you lose to someone like that, I'm not letting you off!"

Clearly, Blue really wanted Red to win. After all, Red had climbed upward by beating him.

Red: "…"

He shot Blue a look—this childhood friend had zero self-awareness.

Another half hour passed.

"Both contestants are ready. Let's give them a warm welcome as they enter the field!"

The host's booming voice echoed through the stadium.

"Red!"

"May!"

Amid roaring cheers, the boy and girl walked out from opposite sides.

"Red will win! Champion Red for the win!"

"I just realized—Kanto's Elite Four plus the Champion actually make up four of the quarterfinalists! And the last one is…?"

"May, you got this! You're the chosen one! You're gonna win!"

"Haha! Red is Master-tier 2. And you are…?"

"I'm betting ten million—Red wins!"

The cheers continued, though Red clearly had more supporters.

This was Indigo Plateau. Compared to long-famous Red, May—who only really began making waves in the later half of the year—had far fewer fans.

Most Hoenn viewers supported her, but far fewer were physically present.

Not that it mattered.

Battles were decided by strength, not popularity.

The referee announced the rules, then left. The pointer on the big screen spun and landed on May.

She would move first.

"My turn, huh."

She didn't care much. She pulled out a Poké Ball and looked at Red.

He stood silently, the brim of his cap hiding his eyes—completely unreadable.

He didn't seem very approachable?

May tilted her head. Rumor said Red barely talked—even at gatherings.

She was lively, but not a complete social butterfly. Should she say something to her opponent?

…nah.

"Yep—trainers speak through battle!"

Her Poké Ball expanded. She raised her arm.

"Elite Red, let me see your strength!"

"My first Pokémon—Breloom!"

She threw the ball hard. A Breloom landed before her—green and beige, standing like a little kangaroo with a mushroom-cap head.

Very Hoenn.

Lillie tilted her head thoughtfully.

"Breloom can be annoying. Spore is a pain," Blue commented.

"But it has a 4× weakness to Flying. Just target it and it drops fast," he added. If it were him, he'd let Pidgeot flatten it instantly.

Who brings a 4×-weak lead? Disrespectful!

"Red's Flying types are Tornadus and Charizard, right?" Lillie asked.

But both were major members of Red's roster—Charizard was his ace. No way he'd send it out so early.

And he didn't need to.

"Venusaur!"

Red sent out Venusaur.

A heavy thud shook the field.

"A favorable matchup," Diantha murmured.

Spore didn't affect Grass types, and Venusaur's Poison typing shrugged off Toxic and Poison Powder.

Even better—Poison beat Grass/Fighting.

But May immediately swapped Breloom for Aggron.

Then she Terastalized—Steel Tera Aggron!

One of the heaviest Pokémon, but held back by its two 4× weaknesses—Fighting and Ground.

With Steel Tera, those vanished.

"Return, Venusaur." Red recalled it.

Sending Venusaur against Tera-Steel Aggron was asking for death.

"Pikachu!"

Red tossed his second ball.

The little electric mouse flipped and landed lightly, cheeks sparking, staring down the brutal Aggron.

Red immediately Terastalized—Electric Tera Pikachu.

"Pikachu, huh?"

May eyed the little mouse, intrigued. Using Pikachu as a major battler was rare.

As far as she knew, only Red and Leaf did that—and Leaf's Pikachu was her ace.

"Haha—sounds fun. Let's see if your Pikachu can beat my Aggron!"

Electric was resisted by Steel.

But May trusted her Aggron.

Like heck a tiny Pikachu could beat a Tera-Steel Aggron—even Red's!

"It's Red's Pikachu," Yellow murmured, eyes wide.

"Pika~"

Her own Pikachu perked up, staring at the field.

"I really don't get why you two love Pikachu so much," Blue muttered. They had actually raised theirs to be strong.

If he remembered correctly, Red's younger brother Ash also used Pikachu as his ace—and it wasn't bad.

"Because Pikachu is cute. And strong," Yellow replied softly.

"Pii~ka~chu~"

Blue rolled his eyes. Strong? That was debatable. Pikachu wasn't rare, and people didn't use them much because they weren't exactly meta-defining.

"By the way, Yellow, your Pikachu is probably an adult now, right?" Dylan asked.

"I think so?" Yellow blinked.

"Ever thought of finding it a mate? I think Red's Pikachu is a great choice."

"Ah?"

"Pika??"

Both Yellow and her Pikachu were stunned.

"Not into that one? Then try Ash's Pikachu. Trust me—Ash's Pikachu has insanely high potential."

Dylan rubbed his chin.

"I mean it—if your Pikachu and one of theirs had a baby Pichu, it'd be a prodigy."

He was certain. With parents like that? The kid couldn't be weak.

Dylan even wanted Yellow's Pikachu to seduce both Red's and Ash's Pikachu.

Have an egg with each—then he could see whose genetics were superior.

"Umm…" Yellow was speechless. But maybe it was time to think about her partner's 'future.'

"Pikachu, what do you think?"

She lifted her Pikachu and asked.

"Pika… pikachu?"

Her Pikachu stared at the battling Pikachu on the field, deep in thought.

"No rush. After the match, I'll take you to meet Ash's Pikachu too. We'll pick a handsome one."

Dylan patted its head.

"If all else fails, I have a Pikachu too. See if you click."

"Once you pick, I'll throw you two a glorious wedding."

And then lay an egg ASAP so he could see whether the baby was a monster.

Was the Gym Leader being too enthusiastic?

Yellow tilted her head. Why was he suddenly pushing matchmaking?

On the field, Pikachu and Aggron were locked in a fierce clash.

May's Aggron was indeed strong—despite its weight and slower speed, its raw power was terrifying. It even forcibly overpowered Pikachu's Thunderbolt at times.

But Aggron was falling behind.

Because Red's Pikachu was too fast—slippery like an eel. Aggron simply couldn't catch it.

And…

"How is it this strong…?"

May's face scrunched up. Pikachu's Volt attacks were matching Aggron's Heavy Slam blow-for-blow, both being knocked back.

This made no sense!

How could a Pikachu brawl evenly with a Tera-Steel Aggron?

Even Raichu would struggle!

How much effort had Red poured into raising this Pikachu…?

P.S. - go check out my new work called Pokémon: Daily Life with Rowlet

---

---

---

# 524: Chapter 526: Please Don’t

"So strong!! Whoa!"

From the stands, Ash was so excited he could barely contain himself as he watched Pikachu fighting Aggron in the arena.

His big brother and the Pikachu he loved most were battling side-by-side on the grandest stage. How could he not be fired up?

"Pikaaa—chuuuu!!"

Ash's Pikachu stared wide-eyed in awe at the senior Pikachu on the field.

"Pikachu, one day we will stand on a stage like this and defeat our opponents too!"

Ash declared this with blazing ambition and confidence.

He had decided: once his journey ended, he would make it into the Masters Eight and shock the world with Pikachu by his side.

Misty, for once, didn't tease him. Brock only smiled. Gary scoffed but didn't deliver his usual sharp remarks.

"Heh… having a goal like that is a good thing."

Professor Oak chuckled approvingly. A pure-hearted Trainer like Ash having a bright future was no surprise to him.

He believed Ash would absolutely accomplish it.

On the field, the battle neared its end. Pikachu was finally struck by Aggron's Iron Tail—but still managed to take down Aggron with Thunder.

Red took the first win.

"Amazing!"

May gasped. Aggron was one of her core battlers, yet it couldn't defeat that little electric mouse.

Absurd—but it only made her fighting spirit burn hotter. That was the kind of opponent she wanted!

Her third Pokémon, Metagross, managed to defeat Pikachu after suffering some damage.

Red sent in Roaring Moon. As a Dark-type, it countered Metagross, but May didn't switch—she trusted her Metagross.

Roaring Moon excelled at close-quarters combat…

But so did Metagross. Would it lose in a melee exchange?

May didn't believe that.

And then—

Her Metagross was smashed by the ferocious Roaring Moon, though Roaring Moon also suffered significant wounds.

May sent in Blaziken next, which defeated Roaring Moon.

The back-and-forth continued, but it was obvious to any keen eye—Red's advantage grew little by little.

When May's Latios finally fell to Red's Tornadus, she was pushed to the brink.

Her ace Swampert mega-evolved, but without rain, Swift Swim couldn't activate—an undeniable disadvantage.

Yet even so, her Mega Swampert showed terrifying strength—surfing upward, landing a barrage of Ice Punches, and knocking out Tornadus.

Such a powerful ace made Red's blood burn. He countered with his own ace: Mega Charizard X.

Mega Charizard X vs. Mega Swampert—two fully evolved starters.

Strictly speaking, Mega Swampert held the advantage. Not because Water beat Fire, but because Ground-type moves struck Mega Charizard X super effectively.

Originally Charizard had Flying-typing, but Mega X changed it to Dragon—making it Ground-weak.

Still, Charizard X hit like a truck in melee.

This battle depended entirely on strategy and the Trainers' rapport with their Pokémon.

The fight was intense. Neither Charizard X nor Mega Swampert yielded an inch—they were stuck in a deadlock.

"This girl is really strong…!"

Leaf couldn't help praising sincerely. She rarely saw Red pushed to this degree.

May was truly exceptional.

"Indeed. But Red is about to win."

Dylan nodded. When Charizard X dodged Mega Swampert's High Horsepower and countered with Dragon Claw, the outcome was sealed.

Both Pokémon were heavily injured—such a strike was fatal.

Charizard X immediately followed up with Dragon Rush. Mega Swampert raised its arms to brace, but couldn't withstand the full force. It was slammed into the ground repeatedly.

BOOM!!

Dust exploded upward.

"Huff… huff…"

Charizard X landed heavily, panting. When the smoke cleared, Mega Swampert lay motionless on the ground.

A flash of light—Swampert was forced out of Mega form and fainted.

"I… lost…"

May stared blankly at her fallen partner and exhaled a helpless sigh.

She fought her hardest.

And still lost.

It hurt.

She accepted the result—but the sting of loss, especially one so rare for her, was hard to swallow.

"Swampert is unable to battle. Charizard X wins," the referee declared.

"May has lost all six Pokémon. The winner is Red! Red advances, May is eliminated!"

"Victory decided! After a fierce battle, Red defeats the powerful May and advances to the semifinals!"

The announcer's passionate voice echoed through Indigo Plateau.

WOOOAAH!!

Thunderous cheers erupted across the stadium.

"Red! Red! RED!!"

"Yes! Two semifinal spots already taken! Kanto is the strongest!"

"Our Kanto 'monsters' aren't losing to anyone else's!"

"What a shame—she was so close."

"Charizard was unbelievably cool. I want to train one too!"

The crowd was deafening, and the livestream was exploding with comments.

"Aah… just a bit more…"

Wallace sighed. If May had won, Hoenn would have had a semifinalist.

"The opponent was simply stronger," Steven murmured.

Even if Mega Swampert defeated Charizard X, Red still had a healthy Venusaur waiting.

Mega Swampert—four times weak to Grass—would never pass that wall.

"If May had Latias' Mega Stone, she could've beaten Red, right?" Wallace asked.

"Who knows." Steven shook his head.

Even if Latias could Mega evolve, Steven wasn't sure it could beat Red.

And realistically, May would never give the Mega slot to Latias.

Swampert was her most trusted partner—even without Mega, it was stronger.

"So, depending on the next matchup, if Hop's friend doesn't get paired against Yellow or Giovanni, then all four semifinalists will be from Kanto."

Steven looked at the display—Red's portrait lit proudly.

"Huh?" Wallace blinked, then realized.

Yes—if Gloria wasn't matched against Yellow or Giovanni, she had zero chance.

She could not beat Dylan.

Her only chance of surviving was matching against Yellow or Giovanni—and even then, her chances were only fifty-fifty at best.

People said Gloria was close to Leon in strength, but Yellow and Giovanni were not weak—both had defeated Lance.

If Yellow fought Giovanni and Gloria fought Dylan, all four semifinalists would inevitably be Kanto trainers.

On the field—

"A great battle. Congratulations on advancing, Red."

May walked up, offering her hand with a bright smile.

"…"

Red lowered his cap, paused, and shook her hand.

"It's only in tournaments like this that I get to meet such strong rivals… too bad my journey ends here."

May smiled wryly at the big screen.

"You're strong," Red said quietly.

"You're stronger this time, Red." She blinked playfully, eyes curving into a radiant smile.

"But next time, I won't lose!"

She clenched her small fist, full of spirit.

"…"

Red didn't answer, but under his hat, his red eyes sparkled with fighting intent.

Two semifinalists were set. The schedule was tight—tomorrow the remaining two matches would begin.

Selene and Red had advanced. Four remained:

Dylan, Giovanni, Yellow, Gloria.

The entire arena held its breath.

"Please, please let Gloria avoid Dylan…"

Gloria muttered prayers for his friend.

Three-in-one odds. Nothing but luck now.

Gloria sat upright, face tense and pale.

All eyes were on the screen as the first portrait locked into place—

Black-haired, sharp-eyed, handsome.

Dylan.

"…It's my turn?"

Dylan raised an eyebrow—not surprised at all.

Now the real question—

Who would be matched against him?

Yellow gripped her clothes nervously.

Even Giovanni stared hard—he desperately didn't want Dylan.

He promised Silver he'd reach the finals.

He could NOT afford to face Dylan now.

(End of Chapter)

# 525: Chapter 527: Core Partners!

"Could it be me?"

Compared to Gloria and Giovanni, Yellow felt differently.

Rather than running into Giovanni or Gloria, she actually wanted to face Dylan. Even though she had already defeated Lance, Yellow understood something very clearly—

The further the tournament went, the stronger the opponents would become.

The pressure would only keep rising. And if she could be matched with her Gym Leader brother right here, then this would be the best possible outcome.

Finally, under everyone's gaze, the second portrait on the giant screen slowly came to a stop.

A girl wearing a straw hat appeared before everyone's eyes.

It was Yellow.

The stadium instantly filled with a mix of groans and relieved sighs.

"Huh? It's Yellow?"

Lillie cried out in surprise. She was quite close to Yellow and had hoped Yellow could achieve an even better result.

But if she ran into her brother here… then this was as far as Yellow could go.

"What a shame."

Leaf sighed as well. Yellow's "cheat-level" victory over Lance had already been shocking enough.

Honestly, Leaf had loved watching that match. If she could see Yellow defeat Giovanni or Gloria too, that would've been even better.

"It's okay~ Being able to battle Gym Leader brother is really wonderful!"

Yellow, however, was beaming. Her pure little face lit up with a bright smile, her big eyes curving into crescents—she was clearly very satisfied with this result.

Satisfied didn't even begin to cover it.

Seeing this, Dylan couldn't help but smile helplessly.

That child really was something else…

Then again, Yellow had never cared much about honor or rankings in the first place.

"So that means Uncle Giovanni is up against Gloria, huh? Feeling confident?" Leaf asked, glancing toward Giovanni.

"Hehe~ That child is very talented, but I believe my chances are still a bit better," Giovanni replied calmly and modestly.

Not being matched with Dylan instantly put his heart at ease.

"Oho~ Uncle's starting to put on airs now. Still, with your ability, we do trust you," Leaf teased.

"Well then, doesn't that mean Kanto's Champion and Elite Four have all made the semifinals? Impressive~!"

Leaf clicked her tongue in amazement, then casually glanced at someone sitting nearby with one leg crossed, munching on sunflower seeds.

Giovanni and Red also cast subtle looks in the same direction.

"Hey, hey, hey! Why are you all looking at me?!"

Blue exploded.

Damn it! He was already miserable enough—did they really have to rub salt in the wound?!

Pfft—

Lillie couldn't hold back her laughter.

"All right! Both trainers are ready. Let us welcome them to the field with a round of enthusiastic applause!"

The slick-haired host's voice rang out powerfully.

"Dylan!"

"Yellow!"

As the names were called, thunderous applause and cheers swept across the stadium.

From the player tunnels on both sides, two figures slowly stepped out. Only when they reached their positions did the cheers gradually subside.

"Dylan versus Yellow—wow, this is going to be good," Rosa said, fully in spectator mode.

After all, this was a master-and-student showdown!

Everyone knew Yellow was Dylan's apprentice, and like him, she possessed the mysterious Viridian Power.

In earlier matches, Yellow had already demonstrated her uniqueness—her Pokémon's strength skyrocketed during battle!

Anyone who had experienced it would vouch for how terrifying it was. Rosa herself included.

"Exactly. I'm really curious what kind of Pokémon Champion Dylan will use today," Hilda said thoughtfully.

Aside from his legends, Dylan was most famous for one thing—

He could use any type lineup.

The man had trained eighteen mono-type teams, each with a first and second squad.

Was he even human?

Hilda seriously suspected Dylan wasn't normal. A person's energy should have limits.

Even trainers like them, who used multi-type teams, already invested far more effort than specialists. But multi-type didn't mean mastering every type—that was an entirely different level.

On the field, the host continued reading from the data card.

"Champion Dylan needs little introduction. Since taking over the Rota Gym from his father last year, he has led it to unprecedented heights, becoming the strongest Champion Kanto has ever seen."

"Champion Dylan's rise can only be described as legendary."

The host smiled.

Most of the audience strongly agreed. Champions like Lance, Steven, Cynthia, and Leon all had stories that could be written as legends.

They were born prodigies.

But Dylan's journey was even more dramatic—and far more inspiring.

"Right after graduating from Pokémon School, he took over the Rota Gym from his father. Many people don't know this, but—"

"When Champion Dylan first assumed control, the gym's reputation was actually quite poor. At the time, his strength was still weak, and Rota Gym was once… uh…"

The host paused mid-sentence.

After all, this was a global broadcast. He couldn't just casually drag another gym into it.

Rota Gym was fine—it was now recognized as the strongest gym in the world, without question.

But that other gym… not so much.

The audience immediately buzzed.

"Seriously? Rota Gym used to be the weakest in Kanto?"

"It's true. I challenged it early last year—Gym Leader Dylan was really inexperienced back then…"

"We still don't know what happened later that caused such a huge change."

"Wasn't it when he started with Poison-types? From that point on, the gym's reputation completely flipped—insanely so."

"I went in with a Dark-type team at first—absolute nightmare! Completely unorthodox!"

The livestream chat filled in the details all on its own.

"Huh? The host said 'Hua—what'?" Ash scratched his head in confusion.

"..."

Brock glanced at Misty, who was rolling her eyes, and sighed.

"He meant Cerulean Gym. The sisters weren't really suited to being trainers, so Cerulean Gym and Rota Gym were both called the weakest in Kanto back then," Misty said, rubbing her forehead.

She wasn't angry—it was simply true.

And she had grown much stronger now. After returning to Cerulean City, she planned to officially take over the gym.

She might not be an elite trainer yet, but she was confident she could change Cerulean Gym's reputation—at the very least, it would be better than when her sisters were in charge.

She had even asked her sisters to look for Gyarados's Mega Stone.

Once she got Mega Gyarados—

She'd show challengers what true Water-type weight really meant!

Dylan had rewritten Rota Gym's reputation. Misty knew she couldn't compare to him—

But she could at least pull Cerulean Gym out of its current embarrassment.

"Ah… right," Ash chuckled awkwardly.

Low emotional intelligence aside, even he knew this was an awkward situation.

Misty didn't mind. She knew Ash too well by now.

The host continued speaking as the referee announced the battle rules.

On the big screen, the indicator stopped on Dylan.

Dylan would move first.

"How unexpected."

Dylan remained unhurried. He calmly loosened his collar, then pulled a black glove from his pocket and slowly put it on.

"Yellow… I didn't expect us to meet here so soon."

He smiled gently at her.

"Gym Leader brother."

Yellow's face flushed slightly, shy yet determined as she met his gaze.

"What you told me back then—I've never forgotten it!"

She gathered her courage.

"I want to stand in front of you. I won't disappoint you—no matter what!"

Yellow knew exactly how her life had changed.

That afternoon, Leaf and Selene had brought her before this man.

Before that, she had been just an ordinary girl—so ordinary she didn't even qualify as a Bug Catcher.

But after meeting this big brother, everything changed.

He took her in, accepted her as a gym apprentice, taught her patiently and gently—and trusted her completely.

Yellow didn't know how to repay him.

All she could do was give everything she had, especially for the scene he hoped to see one day.

Dylan froze.

After a brief pause, he smiled.

"You really are a delightful child."

He looked at the girl opposite him with genuine warmth. Compared to Selene and Silver, Yellow was simply far too well-behaved.

Yellow's smile bloomed even brighter.

"Then let me see what you've gained this past year," Dylan said.

He had many things he wanted to say—but standing here, on this stage, Yellow's words had left him momentarily speechless.

Perhaps the most important thing now was to personally experience her strength.

"Yes!" Yellow answered seriously.

"Then my first Pokémon will be this one."

Dylan took out an Ultra Ball and flicked it forward.

"Drapion—battle ready!"

"Drrra—!"

With a low, menacing roar, the massive scorpion slammed onto the field. Its pincers swung as it fixed Yellow with a predatory glare.

Drapion.

The instant Drapion appeared, Red's eyes sharpened.

"Whoa! It's that Drapion!"

Ash and Misty lit up, memories flooding back to last year.

Ash, in particular, remembered all too well how this Drapion had dealt with Red's Pikachu.

"That Drapion…" Brock sighed, equally nostalgic.

"This was one of my earliest core Pokémon," Dylan said, extending his right hand toward Yellow in an inviting gesture.

"Come, Yellow. Let your teacher see the strength you have now."

# 526: Chapter 528: How a Battle Is Fought

"So it really is that Drapion… Dylan's taking Yellow seriously."

Leaf chuckled softly when Drapion entered the field.

"Huh? Why do you say that?" Selene asked in surprise.

"Drapion was one of my brother's earliest core partners," Lillie replied after a brief hesitation.

"As his strength grew, he stopped using them in regular battles."

After coming to Kanto, Lillie had barely seen Dylan use Drapion at all.

Back then, Drapion and the others were already far too strong for ordinary challengers—using them would've been outright bullying.

And for stronger challengers, Dylan's growing roster of type-specialized teams made appearances more randomized, further reducing Drapion's chances to come out.

"But wasn't Dylan's first rise into the public eye because of that video?" Leaf added with a grin.

"And the opponent in that battle was—"

She glanced teasingly at Red.

Red: "..."

"Yes, that battle video was spectacular. I've seen it too," Giovanni said with a faint smile.

Selene thought for a moment and quickly recalled it—the Vermilion City challenge, right?

"Red, you must want a rematch with Drapion and the others, don't you?" Leaf teased.

For a monster-level trainer like Red, that defeat had surely left a deep impression.

Leaf had only meant it as casual teasing. She hadn't expected Red to respond at all.

But this time—he did.

"…Yes. Very much."

The boy lowered his cap slightly and spoke in a low voice.

Beneath the brim's shadow, his red eyes burned as they fixed on the man in black—and the Drapion before him.

He remembered this Drapion all too clearly.

The one that defeated his Pikachu and Snorlax.

Leaf blinked in surprise.

"Hah~ Then you should tell Dylan. If you two get matched later, have him use that old lineup and fight you again."

Red fell silent.

Drapion

Poison / Dark

The Ogre Scorpion Pokémon

Ability: Sniper

Moves: Crunch, Acupressure, Toxic, Cross Poison, Earthquake, Toxic Spikes…

Description:

Extremely resilient. Even without using poison, it can overwhelm opponents with its raw physical power alone.

"Drapion…"

Yellow gazed at the fierce Pokémon before her.

One of Gym Leader brother's earliest core partners.

That meant he truly valued this battle.

Her heart pounded uncontrollably.

So excited—so excited she could barely breathe.

Her body trembled slightly, not from fear, but from an excitement she couldn't suppress.

She was standing on the battlefield… facing the man she had always admired most.

This was—amazing.

"Arcanine! I'm counting on you!"

Without the slightest hesitation, Yellow sent out her lead Pokémon.

The majestic Arcanine leapt onto the field, mane blazing, eyes sharp as it locked onto Drapion.

"Arcanine!"

Yellow called out.

Arcanine let out a powerful roar, an invisible pressure spreading outward.

Intimidate!

A white shimmer washed over Drapion—its physical attack dropped by one stage.

"That level of weakening won't affect Drapion much, Yellow," Dylan said with a light chuckle.

"Now then—let me see the results of your training."

"Drapion. Earthquake."

Drapion growled, its long tail rising high before slamming down.

The Ground-type attack erupted.

The entire battlefield shook violently as seismic waves rippled outward.

"Arcanine! Extreme Speed!"

Yellow responded instantly.

Arcanine crouched, white light flashing as it burst forward.

Extreme Speed!

Boosted to incredible velocity, Arcanine shot past the seismic waves and slammed headfirst into Drapion's face.

Drapion grunted—but endured the blow.

"Cross Poison!"

Its claws crossed, deep-purple energy blades tearing through the air toward Arcanine as it landed.

Yellow's pupils shrank—that counter was way too fast!

"And Extreme Speed landed, but that's all the reaction there was?!"

"Arcanine, dodge!"

The moment its paws hit the ground, Arcanine sprang backward, narrowly avoiding the slashing blades.

Cross Poison struck the ground and exploded, dust billowing.

But before Yellow could even exhale—

A dark blur shot out of the smoke like lightning.

"Arcanine!"

Yellow's face changed.

The blur resolved into Drapion's tail—clamping tightly around Arcanine's throat and slamming it to the ground.

The iron-like grip locked in place.

Arcanine struggled desperately, limbs kicking—but it couldn't break free.

The crowd erupted.

"Wait—its tail can do that?!"

"That's like Hitmonlee using its legs—this is wild!"

"I grew up in Sinnoh and I know Drapion well, but I never knew this!"

"Not just the tail—Drapion's head can rotate 180 degrees too. Its body is built for combat.

It's just unpopular because it's not pretty and its raw stats aren't flashy."

"Drapion's got Arcanine locked down—what can it even do now?"

Even Ash and the others stared in disbelief.

Ash's Pikachu instinctively rubbed its nearly nonexistent neck.

"Arcanine! Flamethrower!"

Yellow shouted urgently.

"Cross Poison!"

Arcanine strained, turning its head as flames gathered—

But a deep-purple cross-shaped blade slammed straight into its body.

BOOM!

Cross Poison exploded against Arcanine's side. It cried out in pain.

Even without type advantage, even with Drapion's attack lowered—

That hit still hurt.

Then Drapion lifted Arcanine by the tail—

and smashed it down.

THUD!

The ground shook again.

It wasn't even a move, but the sheer force sent pain coursing through Arcanine's body.

"Yellow," Dylan said calmly, glancing from Arcanine to the worried girl beyond it.

"If this is all the strength you have, then it's not what I hoped to see."

"Is this… the powerless, fragile version of yourself you want me to witness?"

Yellow's expression changed instantly.

Of course not.

She took a deep breath, eyes hardening.

"Arcanine!"

Her voice—filled with resolve—seemed to awaken something dormant.

A faint red glow flickered across Arcanine's body.

"Flamethrower!"

Arcanine snapped its head around.

A blazing torrent of fire exploded point-blank into Drapion's face.

BOOM!

The flames detonated. Drapion roared in pain.

In that instant, Arcanine burst free, ripping itself from the tail's grip and retreating rapidly to create distance.

Drapion glared, furious.

It couldn't understand—

Where had that sudden strength come from?

"Here it comes! That's it—that thing again!"

Rosa immediately grabbed Hilda and the others' attention.

She was sure of it—Yellow had activated again.

"Gym Leader brother, please watch closely!"

Yellow's face was solemn, her gaze unwavering.

She would not disappoint him.

She was already standing here—

So she would show him the strongest, best version of herself.

"Ah… then I suppose we'll have to get serious too," Dylan said, grinning.

"Arcanine! Close in with Extreme Speed!"

Yellow stuck to her plan.

This time, Arcanine was stronger.

She believed it wouldn't be caught so easily again.

White light flared as Arcanine dashed forward.

"Drapion—Toxic Spikes!"

Drapion growled, its claws drawing together as a deep-purple sphere condensed.

"Arcanine!"

Yellow's eyes sharpened.

Arcanine accelerated again.

BAM!

Extreme Speed slammed into Drapion's neck.

This time, Drapion let out a pained grunt—the impact was far heavier than before.

But even so, the Toxic Spikes weren't interrupted.

Drapion raised its claws, scattering the spikes into the air.

"Fire Blast!"

If that was the case, Yellow would press the attack.

Using the recoil from Extreme Speed, Arcanine flipped back midair, jaws wide—

A massive, blazing kanji-shaped inferno roared forth.

"Block it!"

Dodging was impossible—but blocking wasn't.

Drapion crossed its claws in front of itself.

Fire Blast crashed down, smashing into the crossed pincers.

Flames roared and scorched, forcing Drapion to grit its teeth under the heat.

"Drapion can't hold that!" Wallace said quickly.

"Right. Arcanine's power has skyrocketed—this Fire Blast is far beyond what Drapion can block without a move," Steven agreed.

But at that moment, Dylan spoke again.

"Throat Chop."

Fire Blast had come just after Toxic Spikes—there hadn't been time for a move at first.

But that brief block had bought Drapion enough time.

Its tail rose high, black light gathering—

And stabbed straight into the inferno.

Throat Chop!

BOOM!

Dark energy collided with raging flames.

Fire Blast detonated violently.

The resulting shockwave blasted both Pokémon backward.

Drapion, closer to the blast, took the heavier hit—but Arcanine wasn't spared either.

The moment Arcanine landed, pain flashed across its face—

Deep purple poison surged upward from its paws, spreading rapidly.

Toxic Spikes—poison damage!

"Yellow," Dylan said with a calm smile, adjusting his collar leisurely,

"Let your teacher give you a personal demonstration."

"This is how a battle is truly fought."

Consider it—

A graduation lesson.

---
